# Engineering constants {#engineering-constants-registry}

This page records the engineering constants of NWQLib: guards, tolerances, safety factors, schedule defaults and work and byte budgets. Each entry gives the value, the site (code location) that defines it, the reason for the value and the condition for revisiting it. The code at that site carries a matching comment ([contribution policy](FRAMEWORK.md#documentation-and-commenting-standard)). Constants taken from papers, such as `(23/48) 4^n` and the Jacobi-Anger coefficients, are documented with their source equations in docstrings. Sites are paths from the repository root.

The entries distinguish derived formulas from untuned defaults. A finite tolerance can define which inputs a numerical check accepts, or a chosen approximation, without certifying the physical result. No default listed here is a reason to add a check of a matrix property that needs the dense matrix.

## Defaults that change your results

These defaults decide the size, accuracy or stopping point of a computation. The linked section gives each value and its reason.

| Defaults | What they decide | Section |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `ExecutionLimits`: `simulator_memory_mb`, `max_total_circuits`, `max_total_shots` | The memory of a local simulation, and the circuits and shots of one Run | [Explicit workflow and reference controls](#explicit-workflow-and-reference-controls) |
| `max_bytes=None` | The byte limit of arrays held in memory, which is then the computer's physical memory, and of files written to disk, which then have no limit | [Shared byte default](#shared-byte-default) |
| `ExpectationMethod.max_products`, `ExpectationMethod.sampling_shots` | Classical work of an expectation value, and the shots per measured group for a requested sampling error | [Expectation](#expectation) |
| Lanczos `krylov_dimension` and overlap cutoff | Size of the Krylov subspace and which projected directions the solve keeps | [Chebyshev Lanczos defaults](#chebyshev-lanczos-defaults) |
| LCHS `approx_tol`, kernel exponent, source-time quadrature and Low–Somma shift | Error allocated to the kernel integral and its quadrature, and the work of the source term | [LCHS defaults and error allocation](#lchs-defaults-and-error-allocation) |
| QPE schedules of QCELS, SPE, RFE and RWPE, automatic time step and `minimum_overlap` | Number of times, draws or steps, the evolution time and the verification overlap | [QPE defaults](#qpe-defaults) |
| QLS target margin, condition-number floor and residual check | The polynomial that approximates the inverse and its phase synthesis | [QLS `1/x` polynomial and phase pipeline](#qls-1x-polynomial-and-phase-pipeline), [QLS construction and readout limits](#qls-construction-and-readout-limits) |
| ADAPT-GCiM `theta`, `t_user`, `max_iterations` and stopping tolerances | Selection rounds, the working basis and when ADAPT stops | [ADAPT-GCiM defaults and tolerances](#adapt-gcim-defaults-and-tolerances) |
| QFD `overlap_cutoff` | Which overlap directions the projected solve keeps | [QFD defaults and limits](#qfd-defaults-and-limits) |
| QHD Method defaults, box refinement and augmented-Lagrangian defaults | Grid, steps, time, schedule, initial state, refinement levels and the constrained outer loop | [QHD Method and refinement defaults](#qhd-method-and-refinement-defaults), [QHD augmented-Lagrangian defaults](#qhd-augmented-lagrangian-defaults) |
| Example notebook sizes and settings | Problem sizes, qubit counts and run times of the examples | [Example instance sizes](#example-instance-sizes) |

## Terms used on this page

| Term | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| admission | The check, made before work starts, that accepts or rejects an input, a planned operation or its data against a limit or a domain. Section and row names and code names such as `AdmissionLimits` use this word |
| work unit | A counted scalar or element operation of a size formula, used by limits such as `max_work`. It is a planning quantity, not measured CPU time |
| formula, law | A formula that gives a gate count, work count or byte count from the problem sizes. Section and row names call it a law |
| allowance, envelope | An amount added to a formula for objects the formula does not count (allowance), or an upper bound (envelope) |
| census | A count of gates, Pauli terms, term pairs or objects that a formula or a check uses, or the table that holds such counts. Code names such as `_census` and `census_structure` use it for the Pauli commutation table, and Lanczos code names and messages use it for the table of Pauli source rows of the Lanczos readout |
| Pauli commutation table | The anticommuting Pauli pairs, one row of later partners per term, and the nested commutation indicators formed from their masks, which a Trotter or QPE error bound uses. The commutator count evaluates that bound from them |
| preparation record | The record (`PreparedArtifact`) that a backend writes about the circuit or classical routine it prepared and executed |
| classical routine | A classical computation that a backend runs in place of a circuit, such as the QHD evolution by `expm_multiply`, recorded as a `KernelApplication`. The [glossary](glossary.md#host-kernel), section names, code names and some rows call it a host kernel or kernel |
| backend instruction | An instruction of a circuit in the form that the backend executes, after translation to its gate set |
| chunk | One part of the observations of a Run, held as an `ObservationChunk`. Elsewhere, a block of entries that a routine processes at one time |
| resource fold | The summation of per-block resource counts into the totals of a Program (`src/nwqlib/resources/fold.py`) |
| run log | The log file in which a Run records its rows (`src/nwqlib/_run_journal.py`). Section names call it the journal |
| identity JSON | The JSON text of a record whose UTF-8 bytes `Record.content_id` hashes ([Record identity JSON](#record-identity-json)) |
| qualified | Checked by measurement on the named interpreter and library versions |
| u | The binary64 unit roundoff, `2**-53` |
| RSS | Resident set size, the physical memory that a process occupies |
| slot | A gate position that a CX or rotation count formula counts ([glossary](glossary.md#slot)), or an address slot of a SELECT, one computational basis state of its address register ([LCHS guide](algorithms/lchs.md#solve-a-linear-ode)) |
| frontier | In `nwqlib.search`, the set of nondominated Plans (the Pareto front) |
| lowering, native, charge | Lowering is circuit construction, the building of the Qiskit circuit of a Program. Native means in the form of the Qiskit circuit or of the backend that runs it. A charge is an error contribution or a counted amount. Section and row names use these words |

## Shared limits

These limits and numerical windows are shared by the Run and by several Methods.

### Explicit workflow and reference controls

| Constant | Value | Site | Reason and revisit condition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `execution.py::ExecutionLimits.simulator_memory_mb` | `None` | `src/nwqlib/execution.py` | Optional local limit. The default uses the computer's physical memory as the simulator allowance, and the width of a simulated circuit has no limit of its own. A 20-qubit complex128 statevector needs 16 MiB. On Aer, a readout other than counts is checked before it is charged, through `AerBackend.admit_simulation_memory`: the live state of `16*2**w` bytes, one more state for amplitudes and `8*2**k` bytes for a probability marginal on k wires. The local NWQ-Sim backend is bounded by its own buffer limit and representable widths, and the Slurm route by its own byte limits. It is an upper bound that the memory a simulation needs is compared with, not memory set aside in advance. Aer receives it as `max_memory_mb`, its cap on quantum-state storage, not a bound on the resident set size (RSS) of the process, and the live and saved simulator states of a trajectory are checked against it before preparation. Physical memory is a capacity estimate, not a reservation of currently free memory. |
| `_prepared_execution.py::Run.wait(poll_interval=1.)` | 1 second | `src/nwqlib/_prepared_execution.py` | Untuned pause between two `resume` passes. Each pass refreshes every pending provider job once, so the interval sets the status-request rate. It has no scientific meaning. Revisit for a provider rate limit or a latency requirement. |
| `execution.py::ExecutionLimits.max_total_circuits` / `max_total_shots` | `None` / `None`, and `execution.py::PAID_BUDGETS` 512 / 1,000,000 | `src/nwqlib/execution.py` | A Run on Aer or the local NWQ-Sim runner, which charge nothing, keeps both unset. A Run on any other backend, which may charge for circuits and shots, fills an unset one from `PAID_BUDGETS` before its run log header is saved (`with_paid_budgets`), and the QHD layers that run several Runs fill them once for the whole layer. The circuit cap applies separately to the number of circuit preparations and to the number of attempts to run circuits. The shot cap bounds the total shots set aside, including those of attempts with an unknown outcome. Attempts that failed, that are set aside but not yet run, or whose outcome is unknown remain counted. Increase either cap for an explicitly intended workload, not to hide a rejected one. |

### Shared byte default

Every `max_bytes` argument and field defaults to `None`, and a Method or record stores `None`, so a Plan has the same content hash on every computer. For arrays held in memory, `None` means the physical memory of the computer that runs the operation, read when the operation checks its known arrays (`src/nwqlib/_limits.py::memory_limit`). This covers input and workspace arrays whose sizes are known before allocation, amplitude access, inspection of prepared circuits and the data that NWQEC and QDK exchange with NWQLib through a child process, which the parent holds in memory. Where the platform does not report its memory, no byte check is made. For data written to disk, a Run folder, a saved Result, a saved `LogicalCompilation` or `PhysicalProjection` and an OpenQASM file, `None` sets no limit, because the physical memory says nothing about disk space. An explicit value applies as given, also above the physical memory. A byte limit is separate from work counts, timeouts and process RSS.

### Byte-accounting conventions

`src/nwqlib/evidence/_work.py::BOOKKEEPING_BYTES` is H0 = 65536, an explicit engineering allowance for scalar bookkeeping, ndarray headers, iterators and fixed sort stacks on the checked 64-bit CPython/NumPy stack. Python objects whose number depends on the input are counted separately. It is not a bound that holds for every interpreter or for the process RSS. Allocator fragmentation, library thread pools and Qiskit synthesis are accounted for separately. `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qhd/binary.py::_integer_storage` is L(b) = 32 + 4 ceil(max(1, b)/30), a conservative byte count for one Python integer of at most b magnitude bits on this stack. CPython uses thirty magnitude bits per four-byte digit, and the 32 covers its header above the observed 28-byte minimum. Numerical arrays use eight-byte float64, int64, uint64 and intp entries, sixteen-byte complex128 entries and one-byte Boolean entries. Saved payload, incremental workspace, peak simultaneous memory and archive size are distinct quantities. B_held denotes the other allocations, already accepted by an earlier check, that stay live in the phase being checked, with a shared buffer counted once. The Pauli plan classification check below uses these conventions. Revisit on another interpreter or NumPy stack, or when measurements qualify these allowances.

### Exact evidence integer representation

Certificates, outward bounds and exact checks compute with `fractions.Fraction` (`src/nwqlib/evidence/_work.py::exact`) and set no limit on the size of its integers. These grow with the binary64 exponents of the inputs, whose denominators are at most 2**1074, and with the number of inputs. A value saved in a record must be representable there. A record writes an integer as a decimal JSON number and holds the integers of at most 4300 characters, sign included, `-10**4299 < value < 10**4300` (`core.records.storable_integer`). This is the range that both readers of a saved record read exactly. `Model.model_validate_json(text)`, which reopens the output of `model_dump_json()`, reads a number of at most 4300 characters with its sign, and Python's `json` module, which the library's Plan and Run folders use, converts at most 4300 digits without the sign. The negative end follows the measured behavior of the locked Pydantic version, and `tests/test_core_records.py` checks both readers at both ends. Within this range, `model_dump_json()` with `Model.model_validate_json(text)`, and the Plan and Run folders, reopen every integer a record accepts exactly. A JSON reader of another language or tool may read a large integer as a floating-point number, which is outside this contract, and Python's `json` reads it at its default settings. A value outside the range is refused when a `Rational` or a Program is built, and the code that produces forecasts and evidence records it as unknown or unavailable with its reason. An Expectation output scaled by a declared `PhysicalScale` 2**e is decided from its binary exponent before 2**e is formed (row "Quadratic-form recovery" below). See [Error evidence internals](error_evidence.md#exact-scalar-arithmetic).

### Numerical guards and tolerances

#### Probability windows of exact execution

`NUMERICAL_RELATION_RTOL = 1e-12` in `src/nwqlib/_validation.py` is a dimensionless input convention with three uses: the tolerance at the endpoint one for probabilities and masses of classical routines (classical computations that a backend runs in place of a circuit) that have no derived window of their own, the floor of the circuit and `expm_multiply` windows below, and subset-mass comparisons. It is not a forward-error bound or a PSD certificate. Unnormalized subset masses scale the relative window by `max(1, whole_mass)`. Revisit for changed precision or units, without enlarging a window in response to a failed check.

`exact_probability_window(G, n, per_operation=c)` in `src/nwqlib/_validation.py` is the allowed gap between one and the sum of the probabilities of one exact binary64 statevector, `max(NUMERICAL_RELATION_RTOL, (c*G + 2**(n+1) + 10)*u)`. G is the backend instruction count in the preparation record (on Aer, the backend instructions that evolve the state for a coherent readout, without save instructions), n the circuit width and u = 2**-53. The preparation record is the record (`PreparedArtifact`) that a backend writes about each circuit it prepares, and a backend instruction is an instruction of the circuit in the form that the backend executes. `PreparedArtifact.probability_window` evaluates the window for one preparation record and takes c from the record's target: `AER_STATEVECTOR_ROUNDOFF_PER_OPERATION`, about 6957, for Aer 0.18.0 and 0.18.2 and `NWQSIM_CPU_SV_ROUNDOFF_PER_OPERATION`, about 164, for NWQ-Sim CPU/SV. Any other target uses Aer's constant, the larger one.

Each constant is a first-order worst-case bound on how much one backend instruction can change the squared state norm through binary64 roundoff. It is derived, not fitted. It assigns to the instruction its worst inexact fold into a fused matrix, its worst applied block and the entry error of a parameterized gate matrix, and doubles the sum for the squared norm. The comment above the constants in `src/nwqlib/_validation.py` derives each term from Higham's gamma_n = n*u/(1-n*u) (*Accuracy and Stability of Numerical Algorithms*, 2nd ed., SIAM 2002, doi:10.1137/1.9780898718027, Lemma 3.1) and the inspected simulator sources. An m-term complex inner product in any summation order has error at most sqrt(2)*gamma_(2m) times the sum of its |products|. Above 14 qubits, its default fusion threshold, Aer folds instructions into fused matrices on at most five qubits. Each fused matrix starts from the identity, so its first fold is exact and G instructions give at most G - 1 inexact folds and at most G applied blocks. Aer's worst fold is a dense five-qubit instruction folded after another operation, about 2896 in 2-norm per unit u. Its worst application is a dense five-qubit fused block, 512, and its gate-entry term is about 70. The NWQ-Sim runner sends only U and CX gates, and NWQ-Sim's fusion (efd0226, `include/circuit_pass/fusion.hpp`) builds gates on at most two qubits, so its terms are about 45, 23 and 14. The readout term 2**(n+1) + 10 covers summing at most 2N amplitude products into a probability total or Pauli expectation for N = 2**n, by any summation tree or a compensated sum, and one multiplication of the state by a global phase. QHD pools the bins of several exact observation chunks (parts of a Run's observations, each an `ObservationChunk`) with running sums and adds `QHDAnalysis.pooled_summation_roundoff`, (M + 2)*u for M pooled bin values, to the largest chunk window before it compares its masses. On the default QCELS schedule of the eight-qubit H4 problem on Aer (nine qubits with the ancilla, qiskit-aer 0.17.2 on macOS arm64), circuits of up to 2,659,881 backend instructions missed normalization by up to 6.1e-12, about 0.02*G*u, which a fixed 1e-12 window rejects. Their windows reach about 2.1e-6, and no miss exceeded 3.6e-5 of its window.

The amplitude-summation term alone is `2*N*u`, where `N=2**q` and `u=2**-53`. At q20 it is about `2.33e-10`, with 16 MiB of complex128 state data. At q50 it is `.25`, while the full `gamma_(2N)` is `1/3`, so the first-order term no longer approximates it closely. At q52 the term reaches one and the gamma formula's denominator is zero. These scalar evaluations illustrate the model's range and establish no backend qualification or large-state allocation. The complete window also needs its instruction and readout terms and the recorded platform assumptions.

The derivation assumes cos, sin and the complex exponential of an imaginary argument are accurate to one ulp. The preparation record's `probability_window_exclusions` lists what it does not bound, and the numeric window is the same with or without exclusions. For Aer the list names supplied matrix, state or channel data (`unitary`, `diagonal`, `multiplexer`, `initialize`, `set_statevector` and noise instructions), whose own unitarity or normalization defect is an input property. It also names mid-circuit `measure` and `reset`, which renormalize the state, control flow, whose executed instruction count is data dependent, and a phase-sum gate whose |parameters| sum exceeds `PHASE_SUM_LIMIT`, as `"<name> phase sum"`. A dense `unitary`, `kraus` or `quantum_channel` on seven or more qubits also exceeds the per-instruction constant. These operations are listed wherever they occur in the backend circuit, including after the readout. An amplitude readout of either simulator lists "amplitude-derived masses", and so does a trajectory of either simulator with a reduction point. A `Reducer` whose own arithmetic bounds those masses resolves the label (`Reducer.state_error_resolutions`, section "Projected scalar reduction"). NWQLib forms the masses recorded with an amplitude observation through BLAS NRM2 (`stable_vector_norm` in `reduce_amplitudes`), whose summation algorithm depends on the installed BLAS, and QHD also decodes the saved amplitudes with NumPy absolute values and sums. Neither mass route is derived here. The QHD tie window (below) bounds only the decoder's individual point probabilities. An Aer release other than 0.18.0 or 0.18.2 lists "unchecked qiskit-aer version". An NWQ-Sim runner whose revision does not descend from efd0226 lists "unchecked NWQ-Sim revision", and a descendant whose derivation sources differ from efd0226 lists "changed NWQ-Sim roundoff sources" (`_ROUNDOFF_SOURCES` and `_ROUNDOFF_BASE_REVISION` in `src/nwqlib/backends/nwqsim.py`). An optimization level other than the adapter's default (0 for NWQ-Sim and IonQ, 1 for IBM and Nexus) lists "optimization_level", also on the record of a counts readout. The error numbers of such a record are a reference, and its operation count describes the compiled circuit. An empty tuple means the whole execution lies inside the derivation, and None, for classical routines and for counts at the default optimization level, means no assessment. Revisit the constants when the Aer or NWQ-Sim version changes its fusion, kernels or gate-matrix construction, or when a library path needs a bound for supplied matrices.

A noiseless Aer statevector execution whose effective simulator method is `statevector`, whose circuit opens with a library direct state preparation (`DirectPreparationGate`, the form in which circuit construction holds a `qiskit.direct` preparation), and whose executed prefix contains that preparation, so that at least one readout point follows it, receives the whole initial state, the prepared vector v on its wires and `|0>` on the others, through one `set_statevector` in place of the preparation circuit (`backends/qiskit_aer.py::_initial_state`). Aer multiplies its initial state by the executed circuit's global phase before the first instruction, and `set_statevector` overwrites that state, so the set vector is v multiplied by the binary64 factor `exp(i*theta)` of that phase (`_set_initial_state`). This product takes the place of Aer's multiplication of `|0>` by the phase, the one global-phase product that `native_state_error` and `exact_readout_roundoff` already count. The factor is NumPy's complex exponential, under the same one-ulp assumption, and the absolute rounding of subnormal components multiplied by it lies outside this relative model, as it does for every instruction. That Aer applies the phase before `set_statevector` overwrites the state was established on examples: without the factor, the exact amplitude readouts of QLS and LCHS test cases differ by a global phase, and with it they pass. An exact Aer readout translates and runs only the instructions before its last readout point, so a readout that observes only at boundary 0 neither sets nor builds the preparation, and the record has no `initial_state_error`. Checked with Aer 0.18.0 on a two-qubit vector whose squared norm exceeds one by 3.6e-12, the saved state equals v in every binary64 word, directly and after a QPY round trip, and a following counts sample draws the same random sequence as without the instruction. These are checks of examples, not of Aer's source. The ideal circuit then starts from v rather than from the unit state `psi = v/||v||_2`. With `S = ||v||_2**2`, `||v - psi||_2 = |S - 1|/(||v||_2 + 1) <= |S - 1|`, and the total probability of v differs from one by `|S - 1|`. `initial_state_defect` in `src/nwqlib/_validation.py` bounds `|S - 1|` from the binary64 sum `S_hat` of the squares of the `m = 2*len(v)` real components. `|S_hat - S| <= gamma_m*S + m*eta` with `eta = 2**-1074` gives `|S - 1| <= (|S_hat - 1| + gamma_m*S_hat + m*eta)/(1 - gamma_m)`, which is evaluated in exact rational arithmetic and rounded upward, a bound on the actual vector. The other operations are unitary and carry this distance unchanged, so the preparation record keeps the bound as `initial_state_error`, and `PreparedArtifact.state_error` and `probability_window` add it, rounded upward, to their terms for the other operations. Those terms remain first-order (`native_state_error`), so the sum is a first-order bound. G counts the `set_statevector` as one operation, one more than it applies. Only this `set_statevector` leaves `probability_window_exclusions`. Any other `set_statevector` and `initialize` remain exclusions, and a direct preparation after the first instruction, or its controlled or inverse form, keeps its circuit. When the live state, the set vector with Aer's copy of it (`32*2**n` bytes) and the saves do not fit `simulator_memory_mb`, the preparation also keeps its circuit. The set vector carries the phase factor and does not give v back bit for bit, and a direct preparation built from it can have other gates. For v = `(1, 2, 3, 5)/sqrt(39)` and phase 0.3, compiled to the basis u, cx at optimization level 0 with Qiskit 2.6.0, the preparation of v has 3 u and 2 CX gates and one built from the set vector has 4 u and 4 CX gates, because its complex amplitudes add phase rotations. The prepared circuit therefore keeps v, a folder Run saves it beside the circuit, and a compiled inventory (`inspect_resources` with transpile options) builds the preparation from v.

The QHD completeness checks and the bound above one use the circuit window (`exact_probability_window`) for probabilities, counts and Pauli expectations, QPE's saved trajectory expectations included, and the saved-state window (`saved_state_probability_window`) for amplitude-derived masses. An unavailable saved-state correction leaves those masses no finite upper bound. The exact LCHS and QLS scalar reduction checks the norm of the complete backend state against the saved-state error of the preparation record that produced it (`PreparedArtifact.saved_state_error`, the qualified error of the backend state propagated through the error bound of the classical phase correction of the saved state), after resolving its amplitude-derived masses label, when that error is available, and otherwise against the same window propagated through that correction (`saved_state_probability_window`), in both cases plus its classical mass allowance (section "Projected scalar reduction"). Aer supplies the phase factor that it keeps as a Python value. NWQ-Sim supplies the factor that its runner reports during preparation and that the execution request carries. The same finite error bound applies to either factor. Multiplication by `(1.0, ±0.0)` adds no error under the assumptions on classical arithmetic. The first-order allowance of the backend state remains part of the resulting saved-state estimate. A record alone does not carry its circuit size, so probability arrays, `PauliValue` and branch masses only require nonnegative or finite values, and QPE `raw_mean` only requires a magnitude above one. `ObservationChunk.validate_unit_bound(receipt)` requires a probability total (the `math.fsum` total computed when the arrays are saved), Pauli magnitude or branch mass of at most one plus the preparation record's window. It runs where an observation is created from backend output and where a standalone Result is saved (`saved_evidence._validate_recorded_data`). QPE bounds `raw_mean` with the same window. QHD analysis (`QHDAnalysis.validate_analysis_masses`) and QLS classical analysis bound their reported masses by the window of the executions that produced them. That is `NUMERICAL_RELATION_RTOL` for a classical routine, except the QHD classical routine, whose recorded `expm_multiply` window (next paragraph) applies.

#### Host-kernel state budgets

`expm_multiply_state_error(calls, start=s)` in `src/nwqlib/_validation.py` is the first-order 2-norm error budget delta of a classical routine that evolves a unit initial state of length D by SciPy's `expm_multiply`, which is Algorithm 3.2 of Al-Mohy and Higham (SIAM J. Sci. Comput. 33 (2011) 488–511, doi:10.1137/100788860). For each call with a Hermitian generator G it takes a bound N on `||G - (trace(G)/D) I||_1` and adds `c` (`expm_multiply_call_roundoff`), and `delta = (s + sum(c + N) + 5*P)*u` for P final phase products. The start term s bounds the construction error of the initial vector (`src/nwqlib/algorithms/qhd/initial_state.py::restricted_state_error`). `state_mass_window(delta, D)` turns delta into the mass window, the allowed deviation of the computed total probability from its model value, `max(NUMERICAL_RELATION_RTOL, 2*delta + delta**2 + (D + 4)*u*(1 + delta)**2)`, and the QHD tie window below uses the same delta. The docstrings derive each term to first order in u under two assumptions that SciPy's own error control makes. [Mathematics](mathematics.md#expm-multiply-error-budget) gives the per-call term, the derivation, the start terms of the initial states and a measured comparison. The QHD classical routine computes N from its schedule and support tables (`method._generator_norms`) and records the mass window as the `probability_window` argument of its `KernelApplication`, the counterpart of the instruction count that a circuit's preparation record holds.

The QHD split-step kernel has its own first-order budget, `split_step.state_error`, relative to the exact split-step product with the recorded step weights, the exact kinetic eigenvalues at the grid's binary64 spacing and the exact sum of the support tables without the objective constant, whose global phase `method._constant_phase` restores after the probabilities are read. With `delta = (s + sum_k rho_k)*u` for the same start term s as above, each step contributes `rho_k = 2*d*5*L + (d + 2)*5 + 15*|dt*a_k|*sum_j max E_j + (M + 1)*|dt*b_k|*W` in units of u, where L is the transform level count (`split_step.transform_levels`), M the number of support tables and `W = sum_S max |v_S|`. The terms are the transform assumption `TRANSFORM_ROUNDOFF`, one `GLOBAL_PHASE_STATE_ROUNDOFF` per phase multiplication, and the formation of the kinetic angles `fl(fl(dt*a)*E)` with E within `EIGENVALUE_ROUNDOFF` units and of the potential angles `fl(fl(dt*b)/2*V_0)` with the table sum `V_0` formed in M - 1 roundings, since `|exp(i*a) - exp(i*b)| <= |a - b|`. The same `state_mass_window` gives its mass window, and `probability_difference_window` of this budget is the upper limit of its tie window. For the tie window, the kernel uses the smaller budget that `split_step.evolve` observes along its trajectory, which assigns each kinetic phase `15*|dt*a_k|*||E_j z||` for the normalized mode populations z after that axis's forward transform and each potential half `|dt*b_k|/2*(2*||V_0 z|| + max(M - 1, 0)*W)` on its own input, with each moment capped at its maximum above. The angle terms dominate when a kinetic integral is large. With `ShiftedCubicSchedule(s=1e-3)`, the integrated rule and a five-point grid of spacing 1/2, the first kinetic angles reach 3e7 rad, the computed state from the kinetic ground state differs from a 30-digit evaluation of the same product by 4.3e-10, the observed budget is 3.6e-9 and the Plan's budget is 5.0e-8, while the transform and multiplication terms alone give 1.9e-14 (Python 3.12.14 and SciPy 1.18.1 on macOS arm64, with the objective `x³/8 - x²/2 + x/4 + 3/8` on the interior grid of [-1.5, 1.5], three steps to T = 0.75 and a 30-digit mpmath evaluation).

#### QHD most probable point and tie window

The QHD most probable point is the lexicographically smallest valid grid point with positive probability whose computed probability p satisfies `M - p <= w` for the computed maximum M (`method._summarize`). The window w bounds how far the computed difference of two probabilities can lie from the difference in the model the path evaluates. For a unit state and a computed state within delta in 2-norm, up to a phase, the operator `|i><i| - |j><j|` has norm one, so the difference moves by at most `2*delta*sqrt(p_i + p_j) + delta**2 <= 2*delta + delta**2`. Evaluating each probability with relative error e adds `e*(1 + delta)**2`, which gives `probability_difference_window(delta, e)` in `src/nwqlib/_validation.py`. `method._readout_window` chooses delta and e by path. The classical routine uses e = 5u (`ABSOLUTE_SQUARE_ROUNDOFF`) with the delta that it saves as its `readout_state_error` scalar, the observed split-step budget above or the Plan budget of the other variants (`method._host_state_error`). Its `KernelApplication` records the window of the Plan budget as `tie_window_ceiling` (`method._host_tie_window`), and the recorded window must not exceed it (`method._executed_host_window`). Exact chunks from a backend circuit take delta from `PreparedArtifact.state_error`, which is `native_state_error(G, c) = (c*G/2 + 5)*u`, half of each doubled per-instruction constant plus one global-phase product, when the target has a derived constant c, the instruction count G is known, the preparation record assessed its `probability_window_exclusions` and the caller resolves every entry. Otherwise the preparation record reports the reason and QHD records no window and compares the computed probabilities exactly. Probabilities from a backend circuit use e = 2u (`COMPONENT_SQUARES_ROUNDOFF`). For C averaged chunks the window is `mean_c E_c + rho_C*mean_c (1 + 2u)(1 + delta_c)**2` with `E_c = probability_difference_window(delta_c, 2u)`, `rho_1 = 0` and `rho_C = C*u/(1 - C*u)`, because a grid point receives at most C contributions of at most C roundings each. Saved amplitudes use e = 5u, and the QHD decoder resolves the preparation record's "amplitude-derived masses" label for these point probabilities only. Their delta is `PreparedArtifact.saved_state_error`, the same budget propagated through the error bound of any classical phase correction of the saved state (`statevector_roundoff`), and a preparation record whose classical correction was not assessed records no window. Counts are pooled as integers and compared exactly, so w = 0. Evaluated from these formulas, not measured, w is 3.2e-13 for one variable with K = 3 and two classical-routine steps, 9.0e-12 and 1.3e-11 for a 3-by-3 grid with 80 classical-routine steps at T = 1 and 10, 3.2e-8 for the two-step K = 39 grid above, and 2.8e-11, 7.6e-11 and 1.5e-10 for exact Aer probabilities of 36, 98 and 190 backend instructions. Each is a first-order worst-case bound, not a measured error. Revisit with the constants it reuses, or when a readout sums several amplitudes into one bin.

The mode representative is the first observed valid grid point with positive weight in lexicographic order whose computed probability deficit from the maximum over the same set of points passes the numerical tie window. Its computed probability can therefore be below the maximum. Read the reported deficit and tie window alongside the point. If the window is unavailable, the implementation compares the computed probabilities exactly and records why the numerical allowance is unavailable. With counts, the comparison uses empirical frequencies with a zero window and gives no sampling-error guarantee.

`mode_status` is `resolved` when the tie rule accepts only one positive observed valid point, `unresolved` when it accepts another, and `unavailable` when there is no positive valid outcome or no derived window. A zero deficit can still have unresolved status.

If every pairwise difference of the computed probabilities has error at most the window, [Proposition 45](mathematics.md#r45) bounds the model-probability deficit of the reported point.

#### Roundoff constants

| Constant | Value | Site | Reason and revisit condition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `AER_STATEVECTOR_ROUNDOFF_PER_OPERATION` | 2*(2896.3 + 512 + 70.3), about 6957, in units of u | `src/nwqlib/_validation.py` | Derived first-order change of the squared state norm per backend instruction of Aer 0.18.0 or 0.18.2: worst five-qubit fold, worst five-qubit applied block and gate-entry term (paragraph on `exact_probability_window` above). Revisit when Aer changes its fusion, kernels or gate matrices. |
| `NWQSIM_CPU_SV_ROUNDOFF_PER_OPERATION` | 2*(45.3 + 22.6 + 14.1), about 164, in units of u | `src/nwqlib/_validation.py` | The same bound for NWQ-Sim CPU/SV at efd0226 and its descendants with the same derivation sources (`_ROUNDOFF_SOURCES` in `src/nwqlib/backends/nwqsim.py`: value type and fusion switch, circuit gate records, gate-matrix storage, fusion pass, U/CX matrices and CPU/SV kernels), whose runner sends only U and CX and whose fusion builds gates on at most two qubits. Revisit when upstream changes those files. |
| `EXPM_MULTIPLY_TERMS_MAX` / `EXPM_MULTIPLY_THETA_MAX` | 55 / 9.9 | `src/nwqlib/_validation.py` | SciPy's `m_max` and the double-precision theta_55 of Al-Mohy and Higham's Table 3.1 (doi:10.1137/100788860), which fix the largest Taylor degree and the substep size of `expm_multiply`. Paper and SciPy constants mirrored for `expm_multiply_call_roundoff`, not tuning choices. Revisit when SciPy changes its `m_max`, theta table or algorithm. |
| `GATE_ENTRY_ERROR` | 10, in units of u | `src/nwqlib/_validation.py` | Entrywise relative error of a parameterized gate matrix under one-ulp cos, sin and complex exponential: at most 5u for Aer's constructions and 9.83u for NWQ-Sim's U gate. Revisit if a simulator composes more factors per entry. |
| `PHASE_SUM_LIMIT` | 4*pi | `src/nwqlib/_validation.py` | Largest sum of absolute parameter values of an Aer phase-sum gate (u, u2, u3, cu, cu2, cu3, mcu, mcu2, mcu3) inside the derivation. It allows three angles reduced to [-pi, pi]. A larger sum is listed as an exclusion instead of widening every window. Revisit if library constructions produce larger unreduced angles. |
| `GLOBAL_PHASE_STATE_ROUNDOFF` | 5, in units of u | `src/nwqlib/_validation.py` | 2-norm change of a unit state multiplied by one global phase exp(i*phi). A one-ulp phase entry contributes 2u and the complex product sqrt(2)*gamma_2, 4.83u in total, rounded up. `exact_readout_roundoff` counts it twice for the squared norm, `native_state_error` once per execution and `expm_multiply_state_error` once per final phase product. Revisit with the complex exponential or complex-product model. |
| `COMPONENT_SQUARES_ROUNDOFF` / `ABSOLUTE_SQUARE_ROUNDOFF` | 2 / 5, in units of u | `src/nwqlib/_validation.py` | Relative error of one probability evaluated from one amplitude, as used by the QHD tie windows and the classical mass window. `re*re + im*im` (Aer full-register probabilities, NWQ-Sim runner probability bins) makes two products and one nonnegative addition, gamma_2. `np.abs(z)**2` (QHD classical routine and amplitude decoder) is `(1 + 2u)**2 (1 + u) - 1` to first order, assuming NumPy's complex absolute value is accurate to 2u for normal nonzero magnitudes. Revisit when a readout sums several amplitudes into one bin or NumPy changes its complex absolute value. |

### Finite search frontier envelope

`nwqlib.search.scan` and `SearchSelection` compare the stored objective values exactly, counts as integers and predicted seconds as the binary64 sums of their forecasts. The Pareto front compares every ordered pair of rows on every objective, `N*(N-1)*k` coordinate comparisons for N rows and k objectives, so its cost is quadratic in the number of Plans supplied, and it keeps ties under strict dominance. Replace the Pareto-front algorithm if this quadratic cost becomes material. Rescoring saved values reads the supplied forecast rows without reevaluating their models, and its original scan counters are not rewritten as new work.

## Inputs

These limits govern reading and converting the matrices, operators, circuits and fermionic input that a Problem supplies.

### Explicit input operation limits

`src/nwqlib/operators/inputs.py::OperatorInput.matvec` counts the work of one action before it runs, D² for dense storage, nnz + D for CSR/CSC and the grouped-action formula of `src/nwqlib/operators/_pauli.py::pauli_action_requirements` for Pauli terms, and refuses an action whose count exceeds an explicit `max_products`. The default `max_products=None` sets no limit, and the action's arrays are still checked against `max_bytes`. For N Pauli state coordinates, L terms and b state columns, the shared action allowance is `(L+g) + 3*L + N*(L+g*b+b) + N*b + L*N*b`, where g bounds the number of distinct flip masks. The 3L preprocessing visits are shared by all columns. The grouped pass visits `N*(L+g*b)` term-phase and group-column entries, with metadata and output initialization counted separately. The check also counts the complete ordered overflow retry and its output reset. Label inputs add `L*(q+1)` decoding work for width q. `_matvec_requirements` uses one column and `g = min(L, N)` from metadata. At `N=2**20`, the term allowance follows this full work formula and the caller's `max_products`. These are conservative operation counts of the numerical routine and its passes, not measured CPU work. `FactorizedOperatorProduct.matvec` and `expand`, `FactorizedHamiltonian.to_pauli` and the MPS contractions take the same optional `max_products`, also `None` by default. Methods that act repeatedly pass their own `max_products`.

[Input cost controls](development/input_contracts.md) lists the byte formulas checked beside them.

### Supplied circuit snapshot estimate

`src/nwqlib/blocks/_qiskit_intake.py::snapshot_circuit` checks a coarse estimate of the bytes of its copy of a supplied circuit against `max_bytes`. An object that the circuit uses more than once is copied and counted once. The estimate has three terms, and each is checked before the allocation it covers.

- Each copied circuit, the supplied circuit and each stored gate definition, costs `SLOT_BYTES = 256` bytes per stored instruction, barriers included, and per qubit. This is the per-instruction allowance of [lowering](#selected-block-lowering-limits), above the measured 115 to 177 bytes of a standard Qiskit gate held in a circuit. It covers the gate objects, labels and scalar parameters of Qiskit's standard gates, which have at most four parameters.
- Each copied gate other than a standard gate costs its numerical data, 16 bytes per scalar parameter and the bytes of each array, such as the matrix of a `UnitaryGate`, the matrices of a `UCGate` and the `iso_data` of an `Isometry`. These grow with the width of the gate rather than with the number of instructions, so the instruction unit does not cover them. The one-byte-per-entry finiteness mask of an array is freed before the array is copied and is never larger than the copy.
- Each annotation modifier costs 256 bytes, and a control modifier also `(count+7)//8` bytes for its control state, which Qiskit may form as `2**count`.

A refusal can come after part of the copy is built, and that part is freed with the error. `src/nwqlib/problems/inputs.py::state_input` checks the qubit term of a circuit, 256 bytes per qubit, plus `(q+8)//8` bytes for the dimension integer `2**q`, before it builds the basis record. The estimate is meant to be within about a factor of two of the size of the copy. It does not include the resident memory of the process or Qiskit synthesis. Revisit the unit if Qiskit's instruction storage changes, and the data term when the supported Qiskit classes or the data they hold change.

### Eigen input conversion and classical preparation

`src/nwqlib/algorithms/_eigen_inputs.py::AUTO_DENSE_PAULI_DIMENSION=16` is the small explicit-dense convenience limit shared by Lanczos, FixedGCIM, ADAPT and the quantum `ExpectationMethod` path. It is not a Problem dimension limit and does not restrict compact Pauli inputs. `src/nwqlib/algorithms/expectation.py::_terms` reads this constant for an explicit dense observable and offers no explicit conversion option, so a larger observable is supplied as Pauli terms or evaluated with classical execution. An explicitly requested dense block transform is checked against `max_bytes` before allocation. It passes its nonzero words to the operator as packed X and Z masks, which are checked against `max_bytes` once their number is known.

Gershgorin normalization forms the row magnitudes directly, zeroes the diagonal magnitudes before the reduction, gives empty compressed rows or columns zero radius, and uses the outward allowance `k*eps/(1-k*eps)` relative to `|a_ii|+r_i`, plus `tiny = 2**-1022`, where `k = 8*n+16`, `n` counts a row's stored off-diagonal values, including explicit zeros, and `eps = 2**-52` is twice the unit roundoff, so the relative allowance is wider than `gamma_k`. A matrix whose largest row extent `max_i(|a_ii|+r_i)` is below `2**-969`, where `tiny` would exceed `2**-53` times that extent, is first multiplied by the power of two that brings the extent into `[1/2, 1)`, which is exact, and its endpoints are multiplied back and rounded outward once more. It covers sequential or pairwise positive summation under round-to-nearest binary64 arithmetic, gradual underflow and a one-ulp hypot assumption. The final half-width covers both outward-rounded endpoints about the stored center. This is a conservative numerical allowance, not a directed-rounding proof. Scalar inputs bypass this margin exactly. No eigensolve estimates the enclosure. Gershgorin frame construction counts `32*N+256*D` additional array-data bytes and a 65,536-byte fixed bookkeeping allowance. Here N includes every stored entry for CSR/CSC, including explicit zeros, and equals D squared for dense storage. The input matrix is excluded. The array formula covers the simultaneous magnitude, coordinate, reduction and endpoint arrays. The fixed allowance is qualified for the declared CPython and NumPy stack and is separate from process RSS and external library workspace. The magnitudes are one float array per stored value, two while a scaled complex input forms them, and the sparse major-coordinate and mask arrays are released before the row endpoints are assembled. Revisit the fixed allowance when the interpreter, NumPy/SciPy or the allocation schedule changes.

`preparation_requirements` counts product expansion as `2*D-2` amplitude products and a supported k-qubit standard gate as at most `D*2**k` products. Custom or opaque circuit action remains unknown. Before preparation, the check of the three-vector recurrence counts the upper bound of its original matvec, the storage of the affine copy, m actions and the scalar reductions. These formulas count known arrays and products, not process RSS or Qiskit decomposition time. Revisit when the numerical routines change.

The explicit `EnergyEndpoint` of the energy-shift check (`src/nwqlib/evidence/energy_shift.py::_operator_table`) stores the float64 real and imaginary parts of the operator, with int64 keys for the rows of a Pauli table or the entries of a sparse matrix. A key holds the X and Z mask words of a Pauli row, or the row and column of a nonzero matrix entry. A dense matrix keeps the smaller of two tables, all D² entries in row-major order without keys when at least half of them are nonzero, and otherwise its nonzero entries with keys. Its byte check is a coarse estimate of the dominant arrays, 6T bytes for a table of T bytes plus one mask byte per entry of a dense matrix. T is `16W+16` bytes per Pauli row with W words per X or Z mask, 32 bytes per stored CSR or CSC entry, and at most 16D² bytes for dense input. 6T covers the gathered arrays and the record's immutable copies. The content hash of the endpoint, or of a record that holds it, reads the digests of the arrays in place and adds no copy of them. The exact relation (`_relation`) checks `(16k+96)U` bytes before it merges the U rows of both tables, whose keys have k int64 words, counting all D² entries of a dense table. For a dense Hermitian matrix of D = 1024 on the checked Python 3.12.14 and NumPy 2.5.2 stack, the traced peak was 34 MB for capturing one endpoint, against the estimate of 102 MB, and 247 MB for the relation of two dense tables, against 268 MB. Revisit if the endpoint storage or the record identity encoding changes.

### Fermionic input from XACC

XACC input (`src/nwqlib/subroutines/xacc.py`) supports at most four ladder operators per term (two-body Hamiltonians), bounding the Jordan–Wigner expansion to at most 16 branches per merged term. Revisit for an explicit higher-body workload and its resource bounds. Caller-supplied `num_modes` bounds written indices before expansion. XACC normal ordering keeps every nonzero accumulated float64 coefficient. The final Pauli `coefficient_cutoff` defaults to zero and is explicit when pruning is wanted. Callers that build fermionic operator pools keep their `1e-12` defaults (`GENERATOR_COEFFICIENT_CUTOFF`), applied after the stable merging of terms. The buckets that merge normal-ordered terms and Pauli terms keep O(contributions) scalar workspace until each stable sum is complete, rather than only the final O(unique labels) coefficients. Pruning individual contributions would change the kept operator. Revisit storage only with an equally accurate summation method and an explicit workspace budget.

### Operator and input representation sizes {#numerical-choices-and-representation-sizes}

| Constant | Value | Site | Reason and revisit condition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Original Hermitian input / dense unitary predicates | Exact Hermitian equality. The unitarity check of a dense matrix uses the entrywise absolute `atol` of `is_unitary`, 1e-8 by default | `src/nwqlib/problems/records.py`, `src/nwqlib/subroutines/_matrix_checks.py` | Generic Eigenproblem rejects a non-Hermitian original input. The caller can explicitly symmetrize it. The unitarity check of dense matrices and the computed projected-Gram roundoff have separate domains. |
| `subroutines/_matrix_checks.py::is_unitary` | `atol=1e-8`, `rtol=0` | `src/nwqlib/subroutines/_matrix_checks.py` | Untuned entrywise window for `U†U-I` in explicit dense-unitary builders. Unitarity fixes the operator scale, so the tolerance is dimensionless. The dense unitary input of QPE (`src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/powers.py::polar_base`) applies the window through the computed singular values of its one SVD, accepting `||U†U-I||_2 <= atol`, refusing `||U†U-I||_2 > D*atol` and calling `is_unitary` in between, so the decision can differ from the entrywise test only for a defect within the rounding error of `atol` or `D*atol`. Other callers apply the entrywise test directly. This dense predicate is not used to check the Hermitian input that structured QSP assumes. Revisit condition: Precision, accepted matrix dimensions or a derived arithmetic error bound. |
| `_PAULI_TILE` | 1024 rows or amplitudes | `src/nwqlib/operators/_pauli.py` | Tile length C of the label decoder, the candidate blocks of `PauliTerms.product` and `PauliTerms.group` and the coordinate tiles of `apply_terms`. For N state coordinates, b columns and L terms, put t = min(N, C) and supply a bound g on the number of distinct flip masks. `pauli_action_requirements` counts `a*N*b + 254*L + 1282 + max(9*L, (64+32*b)*t)` bytes, with a = 33 for an already complex128 input and a = 49 when conversion is possible. Grouped tiles use at most `(64+16*b)*t` explicit scratch bytes and an ordered overflow retry uses `(64+32*b)*t`. The preceding AND/popcount pass holds 9L bytes and releases them before either tile phase. Label inputs add 32L packed-array bytes that remain live through preprocessing and action. The 254*L + 1282 bytes bound the Python dict of member lists and the list of rotated coefficients that group the rows, as tracemalloc measured them on CPython 3.12 with one group per row (row Projected reduction workspace). The count excludes NumPy-internal workspace and RSS. Changing C changes storage, not labels, order or scientific records. Revisit with a changed workspace budget or data representation of the routine, or when the grouping of `apply_terms` or the Python version changes. |
| `operators/refinement.py::_scalar_frontier` | 32 integers of `3174+bit_length(count)` bits | `src/nwqlib/operators/refinement.py` | A sum of count finite binary64 values has a numerator of at most `2098+bit_length(count)` bits over the common denominator `2**1074`. Two more bits cover the Gershgorin radius and the diagonal offset, and a cross comparison adds 1074. At most 29 such integers are alive at once, the 22 integers of eleven live Fractions in the sparse Gershgorin scan and seven temporaries of one CPython Fraction addition or subtraction. The Pauli scan peaks at 19. Thirty-two integers cover both. Integers inside C-level arithmetic and Python object overhead are excluded. Revisit condition: A changed scan algorithm, exact-scalar representation or CPython Fraction arithmetic. |
| `algorithms/_eigen_inputs.py` and `operators/_pauli.py::pauli_coefficient_masks` | `64*D**2` bytes for the transform, then `32*D**2+(48+16*W)*R` bytes for the masks of R nonzero words, `W = ceil(q/64)` | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/_eigen_inputs.py`, `src/nwqlib/operators/_pauli.py` | The transform holds four complex128 matrix-sized arrays: the padded matrix, two active levels and the componentwise temporaries of one level. R is known only after the transform. Packing then holds the matrix, the coefficient vector, and per word the flat index, two mask rows, the selected coefficient and the digit temporaries. Under Python's tracemalloc, an 8-qubit dense Hermitian matrix with `R = D**2` peaked at `48*D**2` bytes during the transform and `64*D**2` bytes during packing, beyond the caller's matrix. Labels are decoded only where a record stores them, under the label bytes of `PauliTerms.labels`. Python object overhead and vendor workspace are excluded. The caller opts into larger or sparse-to-dense conversions. Revisit condition: Transform storage or mask representation changes. |

## Programs and lowering

These limits bound the Programs that Methods build, the Qiskit circuits built from them and the circuit-free resource counts.

### Shared Program admission limits

`src/nwqlib/ir/expressions.py::AdmissionLimits` limits the depth of a Program's references to 128, also the fixed limit of the recursive lifecycle check. Its integers are those a saved record can hold, `-10**4299 < value < 10**4300` ([Exact evidence integer representation](#exact-evidence-integer-representation)). The definitions and declarations, the kept fields and the expression and lifecycle steps of one check grow linearly with a Program that is already in memory and have no limit, because a limit would only refuse large valid Programs. Repeat counts and range lengths are not expanded. The work check counts, before they occur, the distinct scans of context widths, the entries of context and cache collections and the processing of correlation members. Identical partitions are reused, genuinely different branch partitions traverse their finite member incidences once, and per-check interning of immutable correlation sets avoids duplicate storage and repeated full-set equality comparisons. These are metadata work counts, not CPU-cycle or process-memory predictions.

The resource fold, which sums the per-block resource counts of a Program into its totals (`src/nwqlib/resources/fold.py`), reuses the Program's AdmissionLimits for its depth, and accepts the integers a saved record can hold, `-10**4299 < value < 10**4300`. It continues the work count of the Program check and reports the total as `WorkloadEstimate.work_units`, which has no limit. The fold repeats the traversal of the accepted Program and carries one cost per count metric, so its work is proportional to its Program. Measured with Qiskit 2.5.2 on macOS arm64, the fold's total work, including the Program check, was 4.7 to 5.9 units per checking step for the QLS Programs of 2D Laplacian grids 4 x 4, 8 x 4 and 8 x 8 (inverse polynomials of degree 59, 103 and 239) and 12.1 to 13.5 for H4 QCELS Programs with the default schedule and with tau = 0.2 and longest times 10 and 40, in the default and CX resource contexts. A saved `WorkloadEstimate` must have `evaluated_contexts <= work_units`, because each evaluated context costs at least one work unit. It checks the integer-growth bounds of the chosen resource formulas before power-of-two construction, and it rejects kept expression definitions whose depth exceeds the limit before returning a WorkloadEstimate. These are work units of metadata processing (a work unit is NWQLib's count of one planned classical operation), not CPU or RSS limits. These limits imply no additional default production benchmark or materialization budget. The four registered CX-count formulas of direct preparation and Pauli SELECT and readout are zero for a zero multiplicity, and the readout formula is zero without system qubits, so the fold returns zero for these without evaluating the formula at any exponent. For an exponent e >= 2, every nonzero formula lies in [max(1, f) 2**(e-1), 32 max(1, f) 2**e) for its factor f, the multiplicity or the number of system qubits. Controlled SELECT, 24 q L - 20 q + 8 L - 14 with L = 2**e, is the widest, and controlled direct PREP is 16 * 2**e - 28. When the lower end reaches 2**14285, a power of two above the storable bound, the fold refuses the count before it forms any power. Otherwise the count has at most six bits more than the bound, and the exact check on the formed value decides. Two calls of a direct preparation on 14,282 qubits, 4 (2**14282 - 2) CX, are therefore saved, and 14,283 qubits are refused. Revisit these bounds if a registered CX-count formula changes.

### Selected block lowering limits

`src/nwqlib/blocks/lowering.py::lower_qiskit` checks every reachable binding and supported Program node, counting Repeat multiplicity arithmetically, before any Qiskit register or gate is built, so a construction with an unbound repetition or an unsupported node is rejected before any register or Qiskit gate exists. The same counting pass counts the instructions that circuit emission will append, one for each BlockCall and one for each register qubit of a Measure or Reset, with Repeat multiplicity. After the structural checks and before any Qiskit gate is built, 256 bytes per instruction are checked against `max_bytes`, whose default `None` is the physical memory, through `src/nwqlib/_limits.py::check_memory`. Emission appends each gate with `copy=False`, so the instruction list dominates the memory it adds. The gate definitions are counted by their constructors, and the tree of a direct state preparation by the check below. The 256 bytes are the per-instruction allowance that the dense syntheses and `product_formula_select_size` use, above the measured resident cost of 115 to 177 bytes of a standard Qiskit gate held in a circuit. A Run passes no `max_bytes`, so its lowerings use the physical memory. Revisit condition: Qiskit changes the storage of `CircuitData`. Before emission adds control to the built base circuit of a controlled block, circuit construction checks the memory of the base's exact syntheses and of Qiskit's control of them against `max_bytes` (`_dense_synthesis.admit_dense_syntheses`), and in a Run counts their work in `ExecutionTrace.synthesis_work`.

A block that declares a `qiskit.direct` preparation holds it as one `DirectPreparationGate`, whose definition, the direct magnitude/phase tree of `src/nwqlib/subroutines/state_preparation/direct.py`, is built when a backend first reads it, after the check above. These blocks are the PREP leaf built through `prepare_qiskit`, with its controls and adjoints, the ADAPT query reference and the LCHS vector PREP (`src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/native.py::construct_preparation`). On q qubits the tree holds one RY multiplexor per qubit and a phase diagonal of one RZ multiplexor per qubit, and a multiplexor with k controls has at most `2*2**k` gates, so the tree has fewer than `4*2**q + 2*q` instructions, wrappers included. `src/nwqlib/subroutines/state_preparation/direct.py::direct_tree_bytes` counts them at the same 256 bytes, about 1 KiB per amplitude. Before a circuit's preparation is counted, the Run counts one tree for each call of these blocks that the circuit makes, Repeat counts included, from the counting pass that lowering reuses (`src/nwqlib/blocks/lowering.py::_count_definition`), and checks the total against the physical memory (`src/nwqlib/_prepared_execution.py::_admit_direct_preparations`). Blocks of the Plan that the circuit does not call count nothing. Lowering builds the trees of controls and adjoints, so each of their calls counts wherever it lies, in the circuit and in each lowered readout view. The tree of an untransformed preparation is built when the backend translates that occurrence. On the noiseless Aer statevector target and the local NWQ-Sim runner an exact Pauli readout and a trajectory translate only the instructions before the readout position or the last boundary (`src/nwqlib/backends/connection.py::executed_stop`), so only the untransformed calls among them count, summed from the counting pass's subtotals without unrolling a Repeat (`_Count.lazy_bytes_before`). A trajectory on Aer adds the tail and inverse of a view as often as it applies them (`qiskit_aer._view_applications`): the tail once for each group of saves at one boundary through the view, the inverse after each such group except the last of the schedule. NWQ-Sim translates each view's tail once and derives its inverse from that translation, so a trajectory there adds each tail once. Every other readout and backend, the Slurm route included, counts the whole circuit. For NWQ-Sim this rests on preparation without a native runner, in which the adapter truncates and translates the circuit and encodes its gates. It establishes no execution qualification. A static Plan checks every resolved point this way before its first preparation, so a later setting is not refused after earlier ones were counted. A noiseless Aer statevector execution can instead set an untransformed preparation that opens its circuit as the whole initial state ([Probability windows of exact execution](#probability-windows-of-exact-execution)), which needs `48*2**n` bytes for the n >= q qubits of the circuit. `src/nwqlib/backends/qiskit_aer.py::_direct_initial_state_bytes` takes that decision for both the Run's check and the backend, and that one call then counts `48*2**n` bytes instead of its tree. Every other call, including a second call of the same preparation, builds its tree. `DirectPreparationGate` checks its own tree against the physical memory when its definition is built, the one step that every path to the tree passes. ADAPT explicit verification checks the tree of a vector reference against its `max_bytes` before it decomposes the reference. Revisit when Qiskit changes the storage of `CircuitData` or the tree construction changes.

Three uses of the same tree that declare no `qiskit.direct` preparation are bounded by their own checks instead. The LCU coefficient PREP of `src/nwqlib/subroutines/lcu/core.py` is checked by `_admit_lcu`, which counts `16*P*(a+1)` work against `max_work` and 128 bytes per address for P = 2**a addresses and so allows up to 2**18 addresses at a `max_work` of 10**8 and 2**21 at a `max_work` of 10**9. Its default `max_work=None` sets no limit. The band-address PREP of `src/nwqlib/subroutines/block_encoding/banded.py` is checked by `src/nwqlib/subroutines/block_encoding/core.py::_banded_plan`, which counts `64*2**a*(q+16)` bytes and `32*2**a*q**2` work and, with at most 2**q distinct bands, allows up to 2**13 addresses at a `max_work` of 10**8 and 2**16 at an explicit QLS cap of 10**9. The LCHS source-branch preparations inside SELECT (`src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/native.py::_source_preparations`) have the padded dense LCHS dimension D, which the check of the dense input (`src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/selection.py::select_dense`, `64*D**2 + 128*D` bytes) bounds before planning continues, at most 8192 amplitudes at the default `max_bytes`. When the dissipative part L is nonzero, the eigensolve (`D**3 <= max_spectral_work` for the unpadded dimension) reduces this to 512 amplitudes with an explicit max_spectral_work of 10**8. MPS preparations do not use this tree and follow their own MPS bounds.

### Prepared-circuit memory estimate {#prepared-circuit-memory-estimate}

`src/nwqlib/_prepared_execution.py::_admit_circuit_memory` compares `held + kept + temporary + new static definitions` with the Method's `max_bytes`, or with the physical memory when that is `None`, before the charge of a point and before the first new work of a static Run. It is a coarse estimate of the parts that have one, with a target of the order of the measured growth, usually within a factor of two. It is not a bound on process memory. The refusal states what it left out for lack of an estimate, and the comparison stays on for the parts it knows.

| Part | Estimate | How long it stays | Site |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Logical instructions | 256 bytes for each instruction that lowering appends | Until translation ends | `src/nwqlib/blocks/lowering.py::_count_definition` |
| Static gate definitions | The `definition_bytes` of each block reached, once per cache key | The Run | `src/nwqlib/_prepared_execution.py::_held_bytes` |
| Parameter specializations | The call-summed frontier of a point, with its predecessor while the next one forms | Until the next frontier replaces it | `src/nwqlib/blocks/lowering.py::_count_definition` |
| Native circuit | The `native_bytes` of each call, multiplied through `Repeat`, plus `NATIVE_POSITION_BYTES` for each Measure or Reset bit | Until the Run closes, or until `Run.release_native` on Aer | `native_held` in `src/nwqlib/_prepared_execution.py` |

| Constant | Value | Site | Reason and revisit condition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `NATIVE_POSITION_BYTES` | 200 bytes per top-level instruction of a flattened Aer circuit | `src/nwqlib/blocks/selection.py` | The stable footprint growth of a Trotterized QPE circuit, whose instructions are cx, crz and one-qubit gates, was 159 to 217 bytes per instruction (Qiskit 2.6.0, Aer 0.18.0, q=10, `max_time=2`). Other gate classes cost more, so a route with its own measurement passes its own law to `SelectedBlock.bind`. Other backends have no calibration of their own and use the same count. Revisit with another Qiskit or Aer storage of circuits. |
| `_CONTROLLED_POSITIONS` | Instructions of one gate under 0, 1, 2 and 3 closed controls, measured gate by gate | `src/nwqlib/blocks/selection.py` | Aer 0.18.0's translation of Qiskit 2.6.0's control, on the statevector target. Control does not scale every gate alike, so a count of the uncontrolled gate or of its CX gates does not give this number. Another backend's instructions were not measured. |
| LCHS periodic `_NATIVE_POSITION_BYTES` | 1024 bytes per instruction | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/periodic.py` | Measured 1.6, 1.1 and 0.98 KB of resident growth per instruction at q=10 and T=1, 3 and 10. |
| QLS `_NATIVE_POSITION_BYTES` | 1500 bytes per instruction | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qls/quantum.py` | Measured 1295 to 1394 bytes of resident growth per instruction on the periodic stencil at q=8. |
| QLS shortcut workspace | `128*a*a + 192*a + 32*n` bytes for the shortcut action, `16*a*a + 64*h*h + 224*h + 32*n` for its dilation, with n the encoded dimension, a = 2n and h = 2a | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qls/host_planning.py` | The shortcut actions count LAPACK's internal workspace, the part that dominates them. |
| QHD `ONEHOT_POSITION_BYTES` / `BINARY_POSITION_BYTES` | 1024 / 2048 bytes per instruction of a whole circuit at the peak of its construction and lowering | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qhd/native.py` | Fresh-process footprint growth was 972 to 1208 bytes per instruction for one-hot K = 20 and 26 up to 10^7 instructions, and 1604 to 2740 for binary K = 256 and K = 64, d = 2. |
| QPE and QFD step law | `NATIVE_POSITION_BYTES` times the instructions of the emitted step, for both `native_bytes` and `definition_bytes` | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/powers.py::step_memory_bytes`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qfd/construction.py` | Each law equals the Aer lowering of the built step on its tested cases. A dense QPE power counts `176*M**2 + 16384` bytes for its M-square matrix (`src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/powers.py::dense_memory_bytes`). |
| Sampled LCHS `_ROW_HANDLE_BYTES` | 32 KiB per row | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/sampled_plan.py` | The handle, preparation record and values of a prepared row, which dominate a short row. Sampled LCHS Runs grew 31 to 34 KB per row, linearly up to 10^5 rows ([Release prepared circuits](prepared_execution.md#release-prepared-circuits-in-a-long-run)). |
| Deferred template allowance | `3*B + 2*B_before` added to the temporary bytes of a new template, with `B = 16384 + 256*N + 1024*C` for N instructions and C CRZ entries | `src/nwqlib/_prepared_execution.py::_admit_template_rows`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/select_synthesis.py::_select_circuit_bytes` | A coarse allowance for the built root, the parameter-assigned target copy and the new gate definition of one substituted call, with the preceding template's definition and translated definition. It is not a claim that three equal physical copies exist or that these containers exhaust the peak. |

The measured growth below is the in-process lifetime peak after `prepare`, before any circuit copy is inspected, minus the footprint before `prepare` (Python 3.12.14, NumPy 2.5.2, SciPy 1.18.1, Qiskit 2.6.0, Aer 0.18.0, macOS, `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1`). QHD's table calibrates the total preparation peak, not its native and definition parts separately.

| Route and size | Estimate (bytes) | Measured growth (bytes) | Measured/estimate |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| QPE, q=10, 18 circuits, `max_time` 2 | 116,333,904 | 156,238,016 | 1.34 |
| QPE, same, `max_time` 4 | 328,931,152 | 353,157,432 | 1.07 |
| QPE, same, `max_time` 16 | 2,628,752,464 | 2,305,345,432 | 0.88 |
| QFD, 12 circuits | 121,632,608 | 146,505,968 | 1.20 |
| QHD one-hot, K=20, N=1000 | 442,543,104 | 512,311,704 | 1.16 |
| QHD one-hot, K=20, N=4000 | 1,769,647,104 | 1,896,089,160 | 1.07 |
| QHD one-hot, K=20, N=8000 | 3,539,119,104 | 3,516,025,640 | 0.99 |
| QHD binary, K=256, N=200 | 658,252,131 | 532,906,392 | 0.81 |
| LCHS periodic, q=10, T=1 | 85,239,552 | 122,208,448 | 1.43 |
| LCHS periodic, q=10, T=3 | 417,019,904 | 436,453,784 | 1.05 |
| LCHS periodic, q=10, T=10 | 2,489,792,000 | 2,356,741,872 | 0.95 |
| QLS, q=8, mass 0.001 | 1,416,627,368 | 1,024,246,624 | 0.72 |
| QLS, q=8, mass 0.0003 | 4,718,260,472 | 3,348,220,712 | 0.71 |
| Sampled LCHS, 8 rows in 5 templates, 64 steps | 6,439,472 | 25,657,344 | 3.98 |
| Sampled LCHS, 8 rows in 5 templates, 256 steps | 24,134,192 | 39,108,656 | 1.62 |

The 64-step ratio includes loading, grouping and other allocations before the template comparison, which dominate at that small size. The comparison itself is cheap at these sizes: about 0.02 s for the QPE rows and 0.31 s for QFD, and for 10,000 sampled rows in 4 templates its stages took 0.111 s of a 10.38 s `prepare`. A custom Method's walk over points and Program grows with their product, and that case was not measured.

These rows do not qualify other routes. The estimate does not cover input preparation bodies and their controlled synthesis, grouping, parameter and skeleton allocations, intermediate backend translation work, IBM's folded circuit classes and bases, eager sampled QSP or custom circuit producers. The predecessor term covers the preceding template of the same request, so a template left in Aer by an earlier request, or an actual predecessor separated by a skipped or reused template, is not related to it. An eager or unsized template states zero, which means no allowance and not zero cost. Request text on a backend other than Aer has no measurement against this law. A reopened Run on such a backend adds nothing for restored circuits ([Run on a backend](prepared_execution.md#memory-of-the-prepared-circuits)).

### Block construction sizes

| Constant | Value | Site | Reason and revisit condition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `blocks/selection.py::select_zero_reflection` | `(q+1)*2**q` work | `src/nwqlib/blocks/selection.py` | Conservative upper bound on the logical construction work of the chosen diagonal reflection, including address-dependent synthesis. It is neither a minimum gate count nor a measured Qiskit runtime. `transform_block` adds the same formula at width q+1, `(q+2)*2**(q+1)`, to the base's `(q+1)*2**q` for a controlled reflection. Revisit condition: The reflection constructor changes. |
| `blocks/selection.py::_signed_pauli_requirements` | `m*(q+16)+32*2**a` bytes and `m*(q+1)+(q+1)*(a+1)*2**a` work | `src/nwqlib/blocks/selection.py` | For m terms on q qubits and a index qubits, the first byte term is the `PauliTerms.labels` formula for the labels that the SELECT constructor materializes during circuit construction. The 32 bytes per address, 32P for P = 2**a addresses, bound the construction's m coefficients and amplitude array together with either two float64 temporaries or the `tobytes` copy. The coefficients are frozen as a read-only float64 array before the amplitudes are built, so coefficients (8m), amplitudes (8P) and at most two float64 temporaries (16m) give 24m+8P <= 32P. Coefficient freezing peaks at 16m before amplitudes exist and amplitude freezing at 8m+16P <= 24P, in sequential phases. This formula counts the construction, not the archive. The work is the reported `construction_work` size formula, in table-entry visits. It counts reading m labels and coefficients and, for each of the q per-qubit UCG tables and the sign diagonal, one pass that builds the table padded to 2**a entries and one pass per address bit. For a UCG table that pass is the dependency test. For the diagonal it is one halving level, whose differences are built as an RZ multiplexor with a Walsh butterfly. The projected copy, the `UCGate` padding and checks, the diagonal's phase wrapping and the butterflies add a bounded number of passes of at most 2**a entries per table and per address bit, so the formula counts these visits up to a small constant factor, not exactly. These are logical allowances, not measured Qiskit synthesis work. Revisit condition: The SELECT constructor, its dependency projection or its coefficient preparation changes. |
| `blocks/encoding.py::_select_planned_encoding` | `32*size*max(1,n)` | `src/nwqlib/blocks/encoding.py` | Conservative table-construction work for structured encodings, with `size=2**a*max(1,n)`. The coefficient 32 is an untuned arithmetic allowance. Dense dilation has its separate cubic formula. Revisit condition: A changed constructor or measured operation accounting. |
| `blocks/_archive.py::write_blocks` | signed Pauli SELECT and readout data `16mw + 16m + 8P` bytes | `src/nwqlib/blocks/_archive.py` | With m terms on q qubits, w = ceil(q/64) and P = 2**a padded amplitudes, the archive saves the packed operator (`B_op` = 16mw + 16m) and one shared amplitude file (8P), plus four NPY headers of at most 256 bytes and the graph and manifest JSON at their written size. Loading derives the coefficients from the restored operator. Shared SELECT/readout payloads and an already written operator are counted once by object identity. The NumPy 2.5.2 NPY writer (`numpy/lib/_format_impl.py::write_array`) writes a contiguous array with at most one data chunk of `min(array.nbytes, 2**24)` bytes. A noncontiguous array can need a second such buffer. Let `B_held,other` bound the other live numerical arrays, excluding this operator, its 8m-byte coefficients and its 8P-byte amplitudes. Let `H_archive` bound the graph serialization and writer bookkeeping for the graph and writer in use. Given those two caller-dependent bounds, contiguous packed and amplitude arrays have the write-phase byte bound `B_held,other + B_op + 8m + 8P + min(max(8mw, 16m, 8P), 2**24) + H_archive`. This expression gives no fixed whole-archive memory limit without values for `B_held,other` and `H_archive`. `write_blocks` takes no byte limit. Revisit condition: The block archive layout or the NPY writer changes. |
| `core/planning.py::Plan._resolve_selection` and `Plan._selected_construction` | one recent entry per operation, two cache entries per Plan | `src/nwqlib/core/planning.py` | Each entry keeps its latest successful pair and requested bindings, and recognizes canonical bindings through the cached Program. A miss replaces that entry before resolving the next point. This bounds the number of cached graphs while preserving adjacent reuse of a point's resolution and construction. Evicted points repeat their normal resolution work and its checks. The cap is an engineering policy and does not bound the bytes or CPU cost of an arbitrarily large point. Revisit condition: A qualified estimate of the bytes of each point, made before its resolution, permits a check against the Method's remaining max_bytes, or measured reuse justifies a larger cache whose bytes are checked. |

### Direct native output allowances

`src/nwqlib/io/qasm.py::export_qasm` sets no default limit on the top-level instructions of the supplied Qiskit circuit, and a given `max_operations` is checked before Qiskit's export against the instructions that circuit inspection counts. With the default `max_text_bytes=None`, returned text is limited to the physical memory and a written file has no limit ([Shared byte default](#shared-byte-default)). Expanded definitions, QASM ASTs and Qiskit workspace remain unknown.

### OpenQASM import estimate

`src/nwqlib/io/materialization.py::materialize_qasm3_file` estimates the memory of Qiskit's OpenQASM 3 import and `UnrollForLoops` as 4096 bytes per top-level instruction after unrolling (`QasmWriteReceipt.unrolled_instructions`), 2048 bytes per qubit and bit of the record's layouts, 512 bytes per byte of text and 16 MiB, and checks it against `max_bytes`, by default the physical memory, before it opens the file. Measured as the growth of peak resident memory in a fresh process with Qiskit 2.6.0, qiskit-qasm3-import 0.6.0 and the OpenQASM parser 1.0.1, a one-qubit HZH call repeated in a loop took 6.2 MB for one call, 86.8 MB for 20,000 and 400.1 MB for 100,000 calls, about 3.9 kB per unrolled instruction. A sequence of 2,000 and 10,000 written calls, 20,084 and 100,084 bytes of text, took 29.0 MB and 88.4 MB, about 350 bytes per byte of text beyond the instructions. A register declared with no gates took about 1.3 to 1.4 kB per qubit and 1.1 to 1.3 kB per bit for 8,192 to 1,048,576 wires, mostly in native allocations that Python's tracemalloc does not see. The estimate is 1.07 to 1.23 times the loop and sequence measurements and 1.6 to 1.8 times the register measurements, except the smallest inputs, where the fixed 16 MiB dominates. Revisit when the importer, the writer subset or Qiskit's circuit storage changes.

### Circuit resource calibration

The caller chooses resource work explicitly as a formula or an inspection of prepared circuits. The matched-workload measurements below document calibration fixtures. They do not choose a resource tier or establish accuracy for a new input.

The independent PF checks are in `tests/test_lchs_resource_structural_law.py`. `test_product_formula_law_matches_recursive_built_select` compares the formula with the CX count of the built SELECT after `decompose(reps=10)`, and the transpiled checks in the same file use basis `cx,u`, optimization level 0 and transpiler seed 7. These fixture counts do not justify circuit construction at a new workload scale.

The PF accuracy rows span address widths 3, 4, and 5, formula-occurrence counts 2, 3, and 10, and measured/block/analytic CX triples `34/34/34`, `90/90/90`, and `422/422/426`. The largest analytic/measured ratio is `426/422 = 1.00948...`. Positive-amplitude PREP pairs use `2*max(0,2**a-2)` CX. The generic complex system preparation adds at most `2*max(0,2**n-2)`. The two-level rows saturate this formula, while the heat row uses a cheaper system-state fast path.

QLS and LCHS-QSP estimates follow the query classes, controls, projector predicates, PREP and reconstruction scales that planning chose. The inspection of prepared circuits uses the same choices. The resource tests compare structural relations and matched counts of Qiskit instructions. Historical compiler counts are not calibration for a changed construction or a portable accuracy interval.

### Clifford and T angle sets
