### NWQ-Sim runner guards

The NWQ-Sim runner sets aside 32 bytes per serialized numeric value when it checks the size of its JSON output, which holds counts and Pauli values. Probability marginals and amplitudes are binary files checked by their exact bytes. This conservatively covers the bundled nlohmann serializer's binary64 and uint64 spellings, in addition to separately counted key and punctuation bytes. Revisit when the numeric representation or serializer changes. The serialized bytes are checked again before writing. Preparation applies the same per-value allowance and the binary file sizes, without the JSON header, before any job is launched or queued (`backends/nwqsim.py::_output_bytes`), so a readout that the runner would certainly refuse is refused there. This does not claim a bound on JSON object overhead or process RSS.

NWQ-Sim's 58-qubit SV and 29-qubit DM guards keep signed 64-bit state indices and the byte counts in NWQ-Sim's C++ integer types representable. The 63-classical-bit guard keeps the runner's projected count key in its unsigned 64-bit container. MPI shot counts are additionally limited to `2**31 - 1`, the public collective's C `int` count. Global U/CX gates also require the local state dimension `2**q / R` to fit that count because their kernels send the full local partition. Local-only gates, global measured bits and global phase do not require this state transfer. MPI rank counts must be powers of two and each rank must own at least two qubits, as required by the public SV_MPI constructor. These representability guards do not promise an allocatable state. Revisit with changed C++ types or a different interface of the MPI collectives or kernels.

The byte formulas of NWQ-Sim's known arrays follow the inspected constructor and sampler allocations: CPU/SV has three double arrays plus one extra double, CPU/DM has two density arrays plus a diagonal cumulative array, MPI/SV has four local double arrays and three full-shot arrays, and GPU SV/DM has two host plus four device state arrays (two have an extra double), with four full-shot arrays. The [NWQ-Sim guide](nwqsim.md) records their per-process formulas and 20-qubit scalar projections. Revisit these formulas when NWQ-Sim's allocation layout changes. They exclude parsed input/circuits, fused gates, communication buffers and additional NWQ-Sim workspace. They are not whole-process RSS bounds.

The runner disables `PURITY_CHECK` before including the CPU kernel. That macro adds a full norm scan after each fused gate and only prints a discrepancy. It is not part of state evolution or required readout. No per-gate reference/diagnostic pass is added by NWQLib execution. Revisit if an explicitly requested diagnostic supplies its own compute budget and a use for its result.

### IBM primitive preparation

`IBMRuntimeBackend` uses resilience level 0 unless the caller explicitly sets another level, so the wrapper does not enable mitigation work by default. The client-side SDK applies the mitigation, twirling and dynamical decoupling options on the computer that runs NWQLib. The metadata of an Estimator job reports the options that the Estimator applied, and each estimate saves that metadata in `provider_metadata_json`. Settings left to the service, such as the device's default `execution.rep_delay`, and provider usage remain unknown. Revisit the option mapping when the supported Runtime API changes.

The layout-mapped observable uses `SparseObservable.simplify(0.)`, where zero is an exact-zero policy, not a numerical tolerance. Qiskit 2.6.0's ordinary `ObservablesArray` coercion simplifies at 1e-8 and can remove nonzero terms. qiskit-mitigation applies that coercion again when the client-side Estimator builds the job, and the estimate omits the terms that it removes there ([Client-side primitives](dependency_issues.md#client-side-primitives)). The already validated sparse representation enters its public prevalidated-array constructor. Before mapping, the run checks `16K(q+Q)+128K` known data bytes for K terms, q logical and Q physical qubits. This covers sparse Pauli/symplectic representations, exact simplification and mapping. It is not the RSS of Qiskit or the allocation of a statevector or dense operator. Revisit if that representation changes.

Preparation writes each of `dynamical_decoupling.enable`, `twirling.enable_gates` and `twirling.enable_measure` that a request leaves unset as an explicit `false` into the options it saves and with which every job of the preparation is built. A counts preparation records a qualified native two-qubit depth when all three are `false`, every operation of the compiled circuit has a known meaning on the device target, the circuit's inspection fits within its byte limit (`nwqlib.backends.inspection.inspect_native_depth`), and, for a template bound to rows of parameter values, the device accepts every value of each parameterized gate. That depth describes the compiled circuit that NWQLib submits, and a requested `max_2q_depth` of FixedGCIM, ADAPT or QFD reads it before each submission. An Estimator preparation never has a qualified depth.

### Resource forecast and explicit inspection bounds

`backends.telemetry` defaults to 100000 comparison pairs of forecast and measured timing. This is a dedicated output cap, and it does not change the scientific model domains.

Explicit circuit inspection limits its known option, inventory and copy metadata to `max_bytes`, by default the physical memory ([Shared byte default](#shared-byte-default)), with an option nesting limit of 64. Its byte bounds are 64 bytes per operation plus 16 per quantum/classical wire for inventory, or 96 bytes per operation plus the same wire term for an auxiliary compiler copy. When that copy builds a directly set Aer preparation from its prepared vector, the vector's 16 bytes per amplitude, known from its number of wires, join the copy's count, which is checked before the copy is made and before a reopened Run reads the saved vector. `src/nwqlib/backends/inspection.py::_options_snapshot` counts each transpile option as `6*len+2` bytes for a string of `len` code points, `max(8, bit_length+2)` for an integer, 32 for None, a Boolean or a float, and `32+16*len` for a list, tuple or dictionary. The total bounds the length of the options written as UTF-8 JSON (`ensure_ascii=False`, the form of the run log and of record content hashes), whose longest form of one code point is the six-byte `\u00XX` escape of a control character. ASCII-escaped JSON can take 12 bytes for a code point outside the Basic Multilingual Plane and is not bounded by this count. The copy of the basis counts 16 bytes per entry plus six per name code point, and the returned inventory 64 bytes plus six per code point for each distinct operation name and 256 bytes for its fixed fields. The option, basis and inventory counts measure the content of those values, not Python object memory. The per-operation and per-wire bounds are untuned allowances for the instruction collections. None of these counts bounds the payloads of installed packages or the compiler's RSS. Revisit with a concrete larger inspected circuit or changed representation. No cap grows automatically. Effective Qiskit optimization level and seed resolve through the installed compiler's configuration rules and are recorded with its version.

The native two-qubit depth of a prepared circuit (`src/nwqlib/backends/inspection.py::inspect_native_depth`) uses the same default, the physical memory, with the same 64 bytes per operation plus 16 per quantum or classical wire. `src/nwqlib/backends/ibm_runtime.py::IBMRuntimeBackend.prepare` computes it with this default for every ISA circuit of a counts preparation whose options allow a qualified depth ([IBM primitive preparation](#ibm-primitive-preparation)), and it has no control to raise it. It does not scan the other ISA circuits, whose records hold an unqualified depth with the reason. Reusing the inspection default keeps one bound for every inspection of a prepared circuit. The computation reads the instruction list at most three times, once to check every operation and once for each of the two depths, and it keeps one depth value per qubit and classical bit. A circuit beyond this bound is not scanned. Its preparation record then holds an unqualified native depth (`NativeDepth.qualified` false) with that reason, so preparation and execution without a depth limit continue, and a requested native two-qubit depth limit refuses the circuit before submission. Revisit when a circuit that a requested depth limit should admit needs more inspection data than the physical memory, which also needs a backend option to raise the bound.

### Explicit logical compilation and physical projection

Explicit `compile_logical` limits the buffers that the calling process holds at once to `max_bytes`, by default the physical memory: the request and the exported QASM2 input that it writes for the child, the captured stdout and stderr with their decoded text, and the result that it reads back. It sets no default limit on the top-level source instructions (`max_operations`) or on the wall time of the child process (`timeout_seconds`). A given `max_operations` is checked before export, and a given `timeout_seconds` stops a hung dependency. The intake of the Qiskit circuit checks the bytes of nested data, and the exported QASM2 input is checked against the same `max_bytes`. Before each captured chunk is kept, the n captured bytes of both pipes are counted together with k·n bytes for their decoded text, where k is 1 while no captured byte is 0x80 or larger, 2 while none is 0xF0 or larger, and 4 otherwise. CPython stores every character of a string in 1, 2 or 4 bytes by its widest character, and UTF-8 decoding with replacement gives at most one character per byte, so k·n bounds the text before it is decoded. The count is exact for ASCII output and about three times the text of an output made only of CJK characters. The diagnostics are decoded once and completely, and the raw bytes are released before four copies of `result.json` are checked against what the request, the input and the decoded text leave. The child counts the QASM text of a full compilation four times against `max_bytes` before it writes its result. At most three copies coexist in the calling process: the decoded text, which the result and the record share, and the record's JSON and its UTF-8 bytes in the final size check. Two coexist while `json.load` reads the result file. The fourth count covers the newlines that JSON escapes in those copies. A count-only result is a short table of gate counts, which the child does not check. These limits do not bound the compiler's internal RSS or synthesis work. Where the platform does not report its memory, `max_bytes=None` sets no byte limit, and only `timeout_seconds` stops a child that keeps printing. A refusal names `max_bytes` or the physical memory. No retries or cap increases are automatic. `LogicalCompilation.save` and `load` set no default limit on the file they write or read.

Explicit `estimate_physical` limits the buffers that the calling process holds for its child, counted as for `compile_logical`, to the physical memory, with no parameter to change it, and sets a deadline only when `timeout_seconds` is given. Its fixed QDK 1.32.3 model uses GateBased error 1e-4, gate and two-qubit gate time 100 ns and measurement time 500 ns, SurfaceCode distances 3, 5 and 7, the published Litinski19 table (Litinski, arXiv:1905.06903v3), PSSPC with 20 T states per rotation and `ccx_magic_states=False`, LatticeSurgery slowdown 1.0 and UnionBound `max_error=0.01`, without graph, post-processing or cache. These are disclosed finite model assumptions, not calibrated optima. The SurfaceCode formulas are QDK's, and [Estimate fault-tolerant resources](fault-tolerant-resources.md#models-and-sources) states which parts of them the papers QDK cites support. Counts-model rotation/CCZ/CCiX zeros require the accepted Clifford+T source. The times and error budget are explicit caller controls, and expanding the model set requires a new decision. The QDK time unit is ns, and public entries multiply by 1e-9 to report seconds.

`estimate_physical` also projects a `WorkloadEstimate` in the `clifford_t` basis whose counts describe one circuit execution, because QDK's `LogicalCounts` describe one execution while an estimate totals its workload. The test is that `settings`, `shots` plus `exact_evaluations`, and `adaptive_rounds` are exact and equal (0, 0, 0) or (1, 1, 0) (`backends/qre.py::_estimated_counts`). Any other population, and an unknown T count, width or measurement count, is refused. The rotations of such an estimate reach QDK as T gates and its rotation count is zero, because PSSPC uses its fixed 20 T states per rotation whatever the requested precision. `resources/records.py::TYPICAL_ROTATION_SYNTHESIS`, selected with a precision eps in the `clifford_t` basis, gives each arbitrary phase on one qubit of a block's gate list `ceil(3*log2(1/eps))` T gates (`resources/fold.py::typical_rotation_t`, exact rational arithmetic). This is the leading term of the typical T count `3*log2(1/eps) + O(log log(1/eps))` of an ancilla-free single-qubit Z rotation (Ross and Selinger, arXiv:1403.2975v3), so the count is an estimate, not an upper bound. The same coefficient is the QHD `T_PER_PRECISION_BIT` below. A phase on more qubits, or a controlled H, Toffoli or CCZ, has no such rule and leaves the T count unknown. Revisit with a qualified synthesis law or with rotation laws for blocks without a gate list.

## Expectation

### Expectation classical work and physical output

`ExpectationMethod.max_products`, when set, bounds known matvec products plus `2*D` reduction products and the shared `preparation_requirements` count before preparation. The known bytes counted before preparation are those of the preparation, the shared matvec upper bound and `32*D` of reduction and input workspace. The cost of opaque Qiskit preparations remains unknown. These are logical counts, not RSS or a preemptive timeout.

Quadratic-form recovery multiplies by the `PhysicalScale` mantissa and binary exponent in exact rationals, so no floating norm square is formed. With `k = e + bit_length(numerator) - bit_length(denominator)` for the scaled value, `k > 1025` always overflows binary64 and `k < -1076` always rounds a nonzero value to zero, and the output is then unavailable without forming 2**e. The sampling allocation replaces C by `||psi||²*C` in the integer-safe Hoeffding calculation and keeps its absolute, sampling-only scope. It refuses a scale whose 2e exceeds the bit length of the storable-integer bound, because the needed shots could not be saved in the Plan, and allocates one shot when 2e is below its negative. No physical total-error or relative-error guarantee is introduced. Revisit for a concrete larger input or action workload.

`ExpectationMethod.sampling_shots` evaluates the shots per QWC group `2*(C/epsilon)**2*log(2*L/delta)`, where L counts the nonzero nonidentity labels, from exact rationals in `Decimal` arithmetic with 80 significant digits. Division and multiplication round upward, and the correctly rounded logarithm is moved up by one unit in the last place, so the computed value exceeds the exact one at any precision. The precision sets only the size of that excess. At 80 digits the relative excess is below 1e-78, so the returned ceiling exceeds the exact ceiling by at most `1 + n*1e-78` shots. For a count below 10**78 that is at most one shot, and only when the exact value lies within a relative 1e-78 below an integer. A larger count can exceed it by many shots. For epsilon = 1e-60 with C = L = 1 and confidence 0.9, the 121-digit count exceeds the exact ceiling, computed at 300 and 400 digits, by 618366286301253102274956874800761038949171 shots, a relative excess of 1.03e-79. The exact value is never an integer, because the logarithm of a positive rational other than one is transcendental. Planning accepts counts below 10**4300, which a saved Plan holds, and one measurement of a setting requests at most `2**63 - 1` shots ([Readout count representation](#readout-count-representation)). Revisit if a count of 10**78 or more must be planned to within one shot.

`src/nwqlib/operators/inputs.py::_scaled_observable_requirements` checks one storage-preserving scaled vector action with `B_scaled = B_action + 32*N + 3*P + 48*K` bytes and `W_scaled = W_action + 2*N + 18*K` work. N is the state dimension, P the original operator payload bytes, and K the stored dense-entry, sparse-nnz or Pauli-term count. `B_action` and `W_action` come from `_matvec_requirements`, including the possible input conversion. For L single-word Pauli terms, `g = min(L, N)` and `t = min(N, _PAULI_TILE)`, its action allowances are `49*N + 254*L + 1282 + max(9*L, 96*t)` bytes and `(L+g) + 3*L + N*(L+g+1) + N + L*N` work. The additional 3P+48K bytes cover invocation-local scaled storage, component scans and the immutable copy of the Pauli terms. The 18K work covers up to four maximum/reduction visits, two ldexp visits, eight mask visits, one initial copy and three immutable Pauli-array copies per entry. The 32N bytes and 2N work cover vector framing and reduction. Pauli coefficient scaling occurs once per operator, so its 3P+48L storage and 18L work are independent of the number of state columns. Classical expectation adds state-preparation allowances. The power-of-two exponent is derived from the largest original real/imaginary component and saved with the scalar frame, and recovery combines exponents before binary64 conversion. These counts cover buffers, the Python containers that group Pauli terms and traversal, excluding other Python object overhead and Qiskit RSS. Revisit when the action, storage-scaling or reduction routines change.

## Lanczos

### Chebyshev Lanczos defaults

Sampled execution uses one coherent physical SELECT setting per odd degree and one reflection setting per unknown even degree. Its cached label-controlled product-basis transform is counted as `SELECT_basis` once per odd setting and per corresponding shot when X/Y rotations are present. Exact probabilities read every requested degree as a point of one walk trajectory. An even degree is read through `PREP^dagger`, the saved index marginal and `PREP`, and an odd degree through the basis change `B`, the saved index and system marginal and `B^dagger`. A tail with `d` backend instructions (instructions in the form that the backend executes) and an inverse with the same count add `2d` instructions to `G`, the instruction count in the preparation record that the backend writes, or `d_forward+d_inverse` when the built inverse has a different count. The final observation needs no inverse when nothing follows it, so each odd point executes the transform once and each odd point before the last point executes its adjoint once. Diagonal I/Z-only SELECT requires no basis transform. The uncompressed input has `O(n L)` two-by-two table entries for `L` Pauli labels and `n` system qubits. This bounds table size, not construction or transpilation time. Address-bit dependency analysis can require `O(n L log L)` bookkeeping, and compilation for the backend has additional unmeasured cost. This is an explicit choice of implementation and cost, not a fitted numerical constant or a claim of minimum compiled gate cost. Revisit with an independently justified readout implementation and its shot, gate, and noise costs.

| Constant | Value | Site | Reason and revisit condition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `krylov_dimension` | `min(8, original_dimension)` when omitted | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lanczos/method.py` | Bounded projected subspace. Neither the dimension nor the initialization proves ground-state overlap. |
| Per-setting stage minimum | 2 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lanczos/numerical.py` | At least two requested observations per pilot or main setting give an empirical variance when they are returned. It does not guarantee how many observations the backend returns. |
| Sensitivity pilot fraction | Explicit caller value in (0,1) | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lanczos/workflow.py` | One pilot and one main stage share the declared total shots, adjusted to the stage minima. There is no default pilot fraction. |
| Sensitivity main floor | min(returned pilot shots, uniform main allocation) | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lanczos/numerical.py` | Nonbinding allocations are kept. Binding floors are set aside first, and the remaining surplus of the donor settings is distributed, with ties broken by stable index. This heuristic keeps a setting from being starved of shots and is not a confidence bound. |
| `overlap_cutoff` / `cutoff_policy` / `noise_multiplier` / `overlap_fail_prob` | None / empirical / 1 / .05 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lanczos/method.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lanczos/numerical.py` | Exact moments use the 1e-12 numerical floor. Sampled default regularization uses an empirical Gram RMS with adjustable multiplier 1, without a coverage or energy-error claim. The conditional Hoeffding Gram bound (doi:10.1080/01621459.1963.10500830) is reported separately. The explicit confidence policy uses twice that bound, so by Weyl's inequality the kept eigenvalues of the population Gram matrix, whose entries are the exact expectation values, exceed the bound. The [Lanczos guide](algorithms/lanczos.md) states the shot requirements and assumptions. Revisit the exploratory threshold and confidence level for the intended workload. |
| Gram input allowance | m*1e-12 deterministic. Zero for sampled input | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lanczos/numerical.py` | Entry resolution delta gives Gram spectral resolution at most m*delta. This is an acceptance rule for inputs, not a certification of the backend's error. |
| Automatic pilot separation | kept minimum and kept/discarded gap above 2*rho_S | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lanczos/numerical.py` | Two moving endpoints can each move by one empirical RMS. A fixed cutoff uses a one-RMS boundary margin. |
| Explicit recurrence normalization admission | 1e-12 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lanczos/numerical.py` | Window for a supplied normalized vector, separate from eigenvalue accuracy. The input that supplies its state as a Qiskit circuit is responsible for its normalization. |
| Projected analysis bytes | `512*m*m+128*m` bytes | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lanczos/numerical.py::admit_projected_analysis`, called by `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lanczos/method.py::Lanczos.plan` and `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lanczos/numerical.py::reconstruct` | Known index, pencil, eigenvector and output storage. The bound depends only on m, so planning checks it against `max_bytes` before any circuit runs. `reconstruct` checks it again against the limit of the Method that runs the analysis, which may differ from the planning Method. Under `SensitivitySampling`, the pilot's solve and its analytic derivative run after the pilot stage and fit the same bound. This is a logical size formula, not a measured process memory. |
| Pauli source-row table and readout constructor | `65536 + M*(16*W+56)` bytes for M source rows and W uint64 words per mask | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lanczos/readout.py::LanczosReadout` | Checked before the constructor allocates the arrays of its table of the M source rows, which the byte-limit error message calls the Lanczos Pauli census. The data formula covers simultaneous support, coefficient, index and sign arrays and the constructor's own row copy. The input Pauli table is excluded. The fixed 65,536-byte allowance covers headers and bookkeeping on a 64-bit CPython/NumPy stack whose versions were not recorded, including empty and small tables. It is not a bound on process RSS. Revisit when the interpreter, NumPy or the allocation schedule of that table changes. |
| SELECT/K copies with mask ingestion | `J*(80*W+88+S(q)) + (q+8)//8 + 1024 + 65536` bytes for J selected rows, W words per mask and `S(q) = src/nwqlib/operators/inputs.py::_pauli_identity_bytes(q)` | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lanczos/method.py::centered_operator_bytes`, `_centered_operator` | One check before the copies are allocated covers the four caller-owned arrays, `J*(16*W+24)` data bytes plus 256 bytes each, live throughout ingestion, the ingestion bound `J*(4*(16*W+16)+S(q)) + (q+8)//8` and 65,536 bytes of other fixed bookkeeping. The ingester then receives the remaining limit. The supplied table and readout are excluded. The 256-byte per-array allowance exceeds the 112-byte rank-one and 128-byte rank-two ndarray headers on a 64-bit CPython/NumPy stack whose versions were not recorded. It is an engineering allowance, not a NumPy guarantee, and does not bound process RSS. Revisit for other array ranks, allocators or interpreter and dependency versions. |

## LCHS

### LCHS defaults and error allocation

| Constant | Value | Site | Reason and revisit condition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| LCHS construction tolerance | .01 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/method.py` | Untuned starting budget for the kernel-integral approximation and quadrature. Input norm, PSD recovery and other error sources determine its effect in physical units. |
| LCHS kernel exponent and component split | beta=.75, half the construction tolerance per component | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/providers.py` | Beta lies in ACL arXiv:2312.03916v2 Sec. 2.2's practical range. Equal budgets separately constrain the cutoff tail and k quadrature. Neither allocation controls every physical-output error component. |
| Composite-Gauss `truncation_multiplier` | 1.0 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/providers.py` | Multiplies the cutoff obtained from the finite ACL Eq. (186) tail budget (arXiv:2312.03916v2). Values at least one preserve the allocated tail bound. The default uses the unexpanded certified cutoff. |
| Constant-source time quadrature | 8 nodes | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/method.py` | Untuned finite Gauss-Legendre rule for the Duhamel integral. It sets work and approximation order, without a default Duhamel error certificate. |
| Low–Somma shift parameter | c=1 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/providers.py` | A working point also used in Low–Somma arXiv:2508.19238v2, Figure 1. The published formula accepts other positive c, whose error and cost must be assessed for the intended workload. Planning rejects a c whose coefficient 1-norm makes binary64 rounding exceed `LCHS_ROUNDING_EPSILON_FRACTION` of the tolerance. |
| `lchs/method.py` | `approx_tol=.01` | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/method.py` | The default pair splits this tolerance equally between tail and k quadrature. QSP synthesis and each automatic Trotter branch separately receive 0.1 times the tolerance. These are component allocations, not a total physical-output budget. The constant-source quadrature is listed above. `resolve_lchs_coefficient_plan` in `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/providers.py` uses the same default. Revisit condition: The requested component accuracy and available branch budget. |
| `LCHS_QSP_EPSILON_FRACTION` (`lchs/native.py`) | 0.1 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/native.py` | Jacobi-Anger allocation as a fraction of the LCHS epsilon. The circuit stage bounds apply the path-specific physical coefficient-state prefactor once to the compensated compiled-QSP bound. |
| `LCHS_TROTTER_EPSILON_FRACTION` (`lchs/native.py`) | 0.1 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/native.py` | Per-node [CSTWZ] budget (doi:10.1103/PhysRevX.11.011020) before physical application weighting. Intentional Pauli-pruning cost is deducted first, and budgeted selection uses the Pauli-triangle structural coefficient once. Fixed-step paths do not evaluate a coefficient by default. |
| `LCHS_ROUNDING_EPSILON_FRACTION` | .1 of `approx_tol`, compared with `u*alpha` | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/providers.py::_admit_coefficient_rounding` | Planning rejects a coefficient table whose 1-norm alpha makes the binary64 rounding scale `u*alpha` (u = 2**-53) of the finite sum exceed this fraction of the construction tolerance. Each term of the sum is rounded at least once, and the exact solution of the shifted problem has norm at most the input norm, so a large alpha means cancellation with rounding of order `u*alpha` times the input norm, plus up to `(N-1)*u*alpha` from recursive summation of N terms (Higham, *Accuracy and Stability of Numerical Algorithms*, 2nd ed., doi:10.1137/1.9780898718027, Eq. (4.4)). The LCHS error model reports floating-point error as unknown, so the kernel and quadrature bounds, which describe the ideal rule with exact nodes, weights and coefficients in exact arithmetic, are reported only when this rounding is small against them. The check is a scale test of the sum's evaluation and does not bound the binary64 construction of the nodes, weights and coefficients, which `native_floating_point` covers. The fraction equals the allowance of each circuit construction stage, `LCHS_QSP_EPSILON_FRACTION` and `LCHS_TROTTER_EPSILON_FRACTION`. It matters for `low_somma_f2`, whose alpha is about `exp(c)*erfc(1/(2*gamma))` (Low and Somma, arXiv:2508.19238v2, Theorem 2). At c = 50 this is about 1.2e16, so `u*alpha` is about 1.3. Revisit with a derived bound for the complete floating-point term. |
| `LCHS_ELLIPSE_STRIP_MARGIN` | .1 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/providers.py` | Keeps the Bernstein ellipse in the analytic strip at distance .1 from kernel singularities. It bounds the kernel by 1/(C_beta*d), not a fit to sampled errors. Revisit only with a justified analytic bound and disclosed cost in nodes and in address slots, the basis states of the SELECT address register. |
| `DEFAULT_PSD_TOLERANCE` / `psd_tol` default | 1e-12, with decision window `psd_tol * max(abs(lambda_min), abs(lambda_max))`, that is `psd_tol * norm(L, 2)` | defined in `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/solution_error_budget.py`. Used by `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/time_independent_terms.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/quantum.py`, and LCHS option records | One scale-aware numerical PSD policy for public decomposition, the choice between classical and quantum evaluation and formula planning. Values inside the window are accepted unshifted. Values below it raise or shift. A backward-stable Hermitian eigensolver returns eigenvalues within `p(n)*u*norm(L, 2)` of the exact ones (LAPACK Users' Guide, 3rd ed., Sec. 4.7), so the window is relative to that norm, and 1e-12 covers `p(n)` up to about 9000. The window has no absolute term, so the decision does not depend on the time unit. Replacing A by `r*A` and T by `T/r`, for r > 0, scales the window and the shift by r. An accepted unshifted violation lets `norm(expm(-A*T), 2)` reach at most `exp(psd_tol*norm(L, 2)*T)` (ACL arXiv:2312.03916v2, Lemma 21, Eq. (162)). The comparison takes constant time once the existing spectral estimate is available. Revisit if supported evolution horizons reach `norm(L, 2)*T` of order `1/psd_tol` or a user requires formal PSD proof. |
| MPS `threshold` defaults / `num_layers` | 1e-14 / 2 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/method.py` | Default TT-SVD threshold and layered-circuit controls. The fidelity of the built circuit requires a separate comparison. |
| `lchs/time_independent_terms.py::_PAULI_COEFFICIENT_ATOL` | 1e-12 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/time_independent_terms.py` | Accepts negligible imaginary parts in identity and angle processing. It removes no coefficient, because the node cutoff is relative (`PAULI_COEFFICIENT_RTOL`). Units are those of the combined coefficient. Revisit against the operator scale and transformation in use. |
| `lchs/time_independent_terms.py::_coefficient_preparation_work` | `W_align = (q+4)*s + (q+2)*u`, `W_nodes = K*(2*m_L + 18*u + 9)` logical visits | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/time_independent_terms.py` | With s = m_L + m_H and u the size of the union of labels, the identity included. Each of K nodes costs `2*m_L + 8*u + 4` combination visits, with its operand-scale scan, and `10*u + 5` for its streamed upper dropped mass (`src/nwqlib/subroutines/trotterization/error_budget.py::upper_dropped_mass`). The SELECT plan counts `(q+2)*u + 10*u + 5` for each distinct node. These units are proxies for the check before running. Revisit with another combination or dropped-mass stream. |
| `lchs/time_independent_terms.py` | `64*eps*comparison_scale` | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/time_independent_terms.py` | Untuned relative comparison window between an existing structural coefficient and an explicitly computed dense coefficient. It introduces no default reference calculation. Revisit condition: A changed coefficient derivation or precision. |

### LCHS work and byte limits

LCHS dense Pauli decomposition uses the block-transform and output-conversion formula in `_admit_pauli_decomposition`. For d = 2^q, it counts `(34*q+28)*d**2+2*H(q)` work and `(104+2*q)*d**2+T(q)` logical payload bytes for the string table. With h = floor(q/2), T sums `j*4**j` and H sums `(j+1)*4**j` over half widths h and q-h, counting an equal half once. At q10, decomposition has 385,888,256 work units (NWQLib's count of planned classical operations) and 130,028,544 bytes. At q11, 1,686,179,840 work exceeds an explicit billion-unit SELECT cap. That cap gives a decomposition-stage maximum of ten qubits, before later coefficient, angle-table and construction work. With 200 nodes, no identity term and p entries in each L/H support, q10 decomposition plus the conservative relaxed SELECT selection permits p=1231 with either 256 or 4096 address slots. Other stages and bytes remain independent.

| Constant | Value | Site | Reason and revisit condition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `lchs/method.py` and `lchs/route_options.py` construction limits | dense SELECT slots `DenseExact.max_select_slots` 4096, work limits `LCHS.max_select_work`, `max_svd_work`, `max_spectral_work`, `max_quadrature_work` and `max_readout_work` `None` (optional caps), product-formula steps `Trotter.max_steps` and `TrotterErrorBudgeted.max_steps` `None` (optional caps) | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/method.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/route_options.py` | Separate limits on the chosen construction and the shared byte limit. Each candidate pair of `lchs_kernel="auto"` is checked against them on its own. A product-formula SELECT stores one angle row per distinct repetition block, so its tables are checked against `max_bytes` and `max_select_work` whatever the step count. The coherent SELECT of `qsp_block_encoding` counts the construction work of every compact-child, supplied-encoding, node-recipe and table query that its generator queries build, at the controls and adjoint each is built with, because those builders check each query against `max_select_work` and `max_bytes` (`src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/parameters.py::_coherent_construction_work`). Sampled QSP planning checks the same queries of its widest branch against `max_bytes`, at the controls that the pair circuit builds them with (`src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/sampled_qsp.py::forecast_qsp`). The q200 diffusion-only periodic example with 270 nodes and r23 has 117,048,003 work and 594,223,488 bytes. Work caps are optional and do not choose extra selection trials or reference computations. Dense slots 4096 give twofold margin over the documented 2048-slot constant-source case, while work and bytes still apply. The slot limit applies to quantum `dense_exact`, where each branch is a controlled dense exponential. `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/providers.py::_build_composite_gauss` refuses a rule whose node count, rounded up to a power of two, exceeds it, before building the rule, and `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/parameters.py::select_parameters` compares the exact address slots of every final layout. The limits set aside no resources and guarantee no practical runtime or execution. Revisit when a larger intended construction has justified size formulas. |
| `SHARED_TARGET_CAPS`, `GENERAL_MARGINS` (`coherent_selection.py`) | caps 0.999, 0.99, 0.9375 and 0.5 of the shared real targets, and margins 1e-3, 1e-2 and 1/16 of the Laurent, one-sided and filter polynomials | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/coherent_selection.py` | The finite levels that `QSPBlockEncoding(route="auto")` compares by CX count before any phase is synthesized. A lower cap or a larger margin enlarges the recovery factor, and a cap closer to one enlarges the ideal discretization bounds of the phase synthesis. The set is an engineering choice, not an optimum over caps or margins. Revisit when the phase synthesis or the emitters change. |
| `SampledPairs.qsp_bank` defaults of LCHS (`sampled_qsp.py::bank_options`) | `buckets` 1 (at most 64), `margin` 1/16, stochastic `cutoff` half the Jacobi–Anger degree, rounded down | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/sampled_qsp.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/sampled_qsp_bank.py` | Untuned defaults. One bank time covers every row with one polynomial per kept term. The margin is the largest of the general margins. Half the degree is an untuned split between the kept terms and the drawn tail, not an optimum. More than 64 buckets would place bank times below the binary64 range of b_max. Revisit when a measured workload needs more bank times or another split. |
| `sampled_qsp.py::BRANCH_ROUNDING`, `SMALLEST_SUBNORMAL` | rho = 32u = 2**-48 and eta = 2**-1074, in `b_max rho + eta (3 b_max + 2 T_e + 1)` | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/sampled_qsp.py` | The rounding term of the inner bias of a sampled QSP Plan, for the branch weights, the scale and combine angles and the scale division of every branch of the support (B23 in the module docstring). Round-to-nearest arithmetic and the 2-ulp hypothesis on libm arccos and atan2 give at most (6 pi + 8.02)u plus second-order terms, below 32u, and the eta term covers weights, times and scales that round in the subnormal range. Revisit condition: A change in how the branch weights or angles are formed, or a libm whose arccos or atan2 error exceeds 2 ulp. |

The analytically specified q10 conditional pulse with 1024 Pauli terms and 256 nodes has selected-table work 397,714,176 and fixed-refinement work 315,897,856 after pair counts, or 583,809,024 at the initial relaxed upper count. Its structural bytes fit the existing limit. These are projections of the stage formulas without a full Plan or execution measurement. Revisit when larger saved-step refinement has justified count and byte formulas. |
| `lchs/refinement.py::LCHSRefinement` limits | dense work, structural work, node evaluations and steps `None` (optional caps), `max_bytes` `None` (the physical memory) | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/refinement.py` | Explicit refinement only, over the model already chosen. Counts bound this requested operation, and no diagnostic loop runs automatically. Revisit with its workload and the evidence kept for it. |
| `lchs/verification.py::LCHSVerification` limits | dense work, node evaluations and PF operations `None` (optional caps), `max_bytes` `None` (the physical memory) | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/verification.py` | Whole-call reference caps. | `_ELLIPSE_CANDIDATE_WORK` | 32 scalar units | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/providers.py` | Conservatively accounts for one candidate's fixed arithmetic/log/exp/order calculation including its single rounding correction. The composite-Gauss provider first completes its scalar panel search. It stops when the minimum node count of every later panel count exceeds the best rule found. It then checks against `max_quadrature_work` the work of the search, 32 units per node of the chosen rule and the quadratic work of constructing the tridiagonal Gauss rule, before allocating the node arrays. A rejection names the complete work counted for the chosen rule. With M candidates and a chosen rule of `total = 2mQ` nodes, the counted work is `32M + 32*total + Q**2`, checked once after the search, so `LCHS.max_quadrature_work` bounds the search work plus the construction of the chosen rule, not every scalar probe. The search tries at most `floor(Q1/2)` candidates after its first valid Q1. It is not a CPU/RSS bound. Revisit if candidate computation changes. |

A sampled pair adds its copy of the SELECT for every segment and the PTSC corrections. 256 bytes is the per-instruction allowance of the dense syntheses for a standard Qiskit gate held in a circuit, whose measured resident cost is 115 to 177 bytes (`src/nwqlib/subroutines/_dense_synthesis.py`). 1024 bytes allows for the controlled-gate object and definition that each controlled RZ of a structured table keeps. Planning and resource estimates do not build the circuit and do not apply this check. Revisit condition: A change in the gates appended per occurrence or in Qiskit's instruction storage. |

The gates and instructions are counted from each branch's step count and labels with Qiskit 2.6.0's synthesis and control. The counted instructions equal those of the controlled definitions in built two-qubit SELECTs with four address rows, both orders and two or three steps, and a branch with a zero global phase emits one instruction fewer than counted. For a two-qubit SELECT with eight address rows and 64 or 256 steps of order 2, construction raised peak resident memory by 153 and 135 bytes per emitted instruction, against 373 counted bytes per instruction. The input preparations of a source layout do not repeat and are not counted. Revisit condition: A change in Qiskit's Pauli-evolution synthesis or multi-controlled gates. |

The gate count bounds a branch of W occurrences per step over R repetitions on q system qubits. Qiskit's decompose holds four copies of the gates at once, the synthesized definition, its DAG, the substituted DAG and the decomposed circuit, each allowed 256 bytes per gate. Explicit branches: `K_eig(8D**3 + 34D**2) + B(D**3 + 2D**2 + 3D)` for B nonzero-time branches plus the work of the chosen synthesis, control and preparation, with `64D**2 + 24D` bytes held before synthesis, the live L and H, cached eigensystems `16D**2 + 8D` each and completed circuits. One Hermitian eigensystem per node, one matvec per input and node, one final action per node and one dense product per explicit branch. The queried LAPACK workspace `Q_V(D)` of NumPy's `zheevd` is not counted. The formulas count the known NumPy arrays and are declared logical workspaces, not complete workspace caps. These units are proxies for the check before running, not timings or equal-cost CPU operations. Revisit condition: A changed eigensolver, branch formation or classical accumulation, or a deployment that qualifies its LAPACK workspace. |

### Explicit RK45 verification tolerance

`LCHSVerification._reference_parameters` rejects `rtol` below `100 * sys.float_info.epsilon`. SciPy's `integrate._ivp.common.validate_tol` otherwise replaces such a request with this float64 floor. Rejecting before reference work keeps the requested and recorded numerical tolerance identical to the one used. This is the domain of the RK45 solver that NWQLib uses, not an accuracy certificate. Revisit if the solver or the supported floating-point dtype changes. The comparison is strict, so the floor itself is accepted, and `tests/test_lchs_verification.py` checks that a smaller requested tolerance is rejected when the options are constructed, before anything runs.

### LCHS circuit-free synthesis laws

These formulas describe the chosen synthesis model and are separate from measured compiler output, like the shared [circuit-free synthesis laws](#circuit-free-synthesis-laws). Revisit them when the construction or compiler changes, using independent matched-workload evidence.

The CX count of a `qsp_block_encoding` route is counted from its emitters without building a circuit, in three parts. The calls of the original block encoding come from `_shared_census` (shared real QSVT, applied three times under oblivious amplitude amplification) or `_qubitized_census` (the two-sided and one-sided Jacobi–Anger walks and the imaginary-time filter) in `src/nwqlib/subroutines/qsp/evolution.py`, and each call is priced at the child encoding's own uncontrolled or singly controlled count (`coherent_selection._query_cx`). The gates that the evolution adds around those calls follow `_shared_gate_cx` and `_qubitized_gate_cx` in the same file: the projector toggles, walk reflections and lift gates are X gates with c controls, counted with `MCX_CX_BY_CONTROLS` below, and each phase slot of the shared route adds one two-entry RZ multiplexor. The coefficient preparation, its inverse, the slot phase diagonal and the reflection follow `coherent_selection._preparation_cx`, with direct preparations priced as in the next paragraph. An X gate outside the tabulated range of controls, or a child without a CX formula, such as a supplied block encoding, leaves the route's count unknown rather than zero. The count describes the emitted construction before transpilation, and route selection compares these counts before any phase is synthesized. Revisit when the QSP emitters, `MCX_CX_BY_CONTROLS` or the Qiskit version changes, and whenever `tests/test_lchs_resource_structural_law.py` fails.

A direct preparation is priced at the costliest construction that `_build_normalized_state_preparation` can take (`compiled_selection._direct_preparation_paths`). X gates prepare a basis state and H gates equal amplitudes, at most n gates either way. The prefix-uniform fast path has the elementary gate-slot bound of `_uniform_superposition_gate_slots` in `src/nwqlib/_preparation_laws.py`, which includes the open CH/CRY control wrappers. The magnitude tree has `2**n - 1` RY and `2**n - 2` CX, and a complex state adds a phase diagonal with as many RZ and CX. Each construction is priced at the control level the SELECT adds, and the largest price counts. A QSP child counts its positive coefficient PREP and the inverse. A constant-source input (`compiled_selection._controlled_direct_preparation_cx`) adds one controlled global phase. Basis, equal, uniform-prefix and general complex states on one to three qubits with 1, 2, 3, 4 and 8 controls stayed at or below the price (`test_controlled_source_preparation_bounds_every_construction`).

The LCHS `dense_exact` SELECT formula (`src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/native.py::dense_branch_select_cx`) counts for each physical branch, on the gate-wise route of `DenseExact.control_route`, the exact synthesis of its q-qubit unitary with every gate controlled on the a address bits, as `qiskit_compat.controlled` and Qiskit 2.6.0's `add_control` build it. On the whole-matrix route, which the default `"auto"` takes for a = 1, a branch on q >= 2 qubits is one whole-matrix synthesis on q + a qubits, at most `(25/96) 4**(q+a) - 2**(q+a) + 4/3` CX, which a Haar-random branch reaches, and a constant-source branch includes its preparation in that synthesis. The gate count of `_dense_synthesis.dense_unitary_circuit` is bounded by `(25/48) 4**q - (3/2) 2**q + 2/3` CX, `7*4**(q-2)` U, `(3/8) 4**q - (3/2) 2**q` RZ and `(4**q - 16)/24` H gates for q >= 2, and one U for q = 1. Controlled on a bits, a CX costs the X with a + 1 controls and an H the X with a controls from `MCX_CX_BY_CONTROLS`. An RZ becomes a multi-controlled RZ (MCRZ) of 2 CX for a = 1 and `2*_dirty_mcx_cx(ceil(a/2)) + 2*_dirty_mcx_cx(floor(a/2))` CX otherwise (`src/nwqlib/subroutines/hamiltonian_evolution/pauli_evolution.py`). A U costs 2 CX for a = 1 and otherwise two MCRZ, one multi-controlled RY (MCRY, 12 CX for a = 2, 20 for a = 3 and the MCRZ count from a = 4) and `_mcphase_cx(a)`, and the branch phase costs `_mcphase_cx(a)`. These are the counts without ancillas. For a from 1 to 8 with one or three idle qubits, clean or already used, no gate kind decomposed into more (`test_controlled_census_prices_bound_qiskit_for_every_kind`). On the gate-wise route, transpiled built SELECT leaves of LCHS plans with q = 1..4 and a = 1..3 (Qiskit 2.6.0, basis `cx,u`, optimization level 0) stayed at or below the formula, with ratios from 0.72 to 1.0 and equality at q = 1 and at q = 2, a = 1. The QSD leading term `(23/48) 4**(q+a)` per branch is below the built count, for example 491 against 752 CX in total for the four branches at q = 2 and a = 2. The value is recorded as an estimate because another Qiskit version can control or decompose the gates differently. For a constant-source layout on the gate-wise route the preparation inside each branch is priced separately by `compiled_selection._controlled_direct_preparation_cx`. With Qiskit 2.5.2, built leaves with a constant source on one to three system qubits and two to eight address bits stayed at or below the total, with ratios from 0.61 to 0.94. Revisit when `src/nwqlib/subroutines/_dense_synthesis.py`, `qiskit_compat.controlled` or the Qiskit version changes, and whenever `tests/test_lchs_resource_structural_law.py` fails.

The branch-controlled product-formula SELECT formula (`src/nwqlib/algorithms/lchs/select_synthesis.py::_branch_controlled_product_formula_resource_law`) prices every gate of every branch at the a address controls (`compiled_selection._controlled_kind_cx`). Qiskit 2.6.0 synthesizes an occurrence of a one-qubit Pauli label as one RZ, RX or RY, and a longer label as an H pair for each X factor, an SX and SXdg pair for each Y factor, `2*(support - 1)` parity CX and one RZ. A parity CX costs the X with a + 1 controls, an H the X with a controls, an SX the MCPhase on a + 1 qubits, an RZ the MCRZ, and a lone RX or RY Qiskit's "noancilla" MCRY, a Gray-code circuit of `3*2**a - 4` CX for a <= 3 and the MCRZ count from a = 4. With Qiskit 2.5.2, transpiled built leaves with one to three system qubits and one to seven address bits, Lie and Suzuki formulas, with and without a constant source, stayed at or below the formula, with ratios from 0.57 to 1.0 and equality for one-qubit labels. The loosest ratios, at three system qubits and four or five address bits, come from Qiskit decomposing the multi-controlled X gates with idle system qubits as ancillas, for example 42 CX for a parity CX under four address controls with one idle qubit, against 84 from the formula at that time. Qiskit 2.6.0 builds the same gate with 42 CX, against 82 from the present formula. The phase contribution of branch-controlled LCHS SELECT uses the same `_mcphase_cx` count as QHD's MCPhase provider. A phase on an a-bit address is a phase gate controlled on a-1 bits. For a-1 <= 4, Qiskit's definition emits MCRZ operations with decreasing control counts. Each MCRZ with at least two controls uses two dirty-MCX pairs, and the one-control MCRZ is a CRZ with two CX. From a-1 = 5, it is `synth_mcp_noaux_sp22`, with `2(a-1) - 1` controlled phase and `2(a-2)**2` controlled RX gates, `4(a-1)**2 - 4(a-1) + 2` CX. A generic diagonal-table formula does not price this constructor, for example at six address bits it gives 62 CX while this MCPhase decomposition has 82. The calculation uses scalar control counts without constructing a gate or a phase table.

## QPE

### QPE defaults

| Constant | Value | Site | Reason and revisit condition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| QPE static schedule defaults | QCELS 16 times/grid 4096, SPE 32 paired draws/grid 4096, RFE 97 paired draws/49 frequencies | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/method.py` | Untuned finite workloads. SPE and RFE make one query per distinct drawn (power, quadrature) and keep its draw multiplicity in the estimate. Exact readout evaluates each query once on one controlled trajectory. Shot-mode SPE and RFE request 64 and 194 times `shots` coherent shots, and a query drawn m times is measured with m times `shots`. At RFE's defaults the expected number of distinct settings is about 84.7. K sets RFE phase spacing 2π/K and maximum power K-1. These defaults do not establish a confidence theorem. |
| SPE Fourier filter | degree d=11, beta=6 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/method.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/numerical.py` | WBC arXiv:2110.12071v2 PDF Eq. (A2), HTML Eq. (17), gives the coefficients. Maximum frequency is 2d+1. With epsilon1=epsilon2=eta/8 and epsilon3=eta/2, Theorem 3 has two assumptions. Its beta assumption, beta >= max(W(2/(pi*epsilon3^2))/(4*sin(delta)^2), 1) with W the principal Lambert W function, gives the smallest transition half-width delta that beta supports, and PDF Eqs. (A6)-(A7), HTML Eqs. (21)-(22), give the degree assumption that `spe_filter_domain` checks for d. The resulting filter bound is 3eta/8, leaving eta/8 below the decision threshold for sampling error. A finite sample count is not automatically sufficient. The overlap lower bound is an assumption that the caller supplies. |
| QCELS finite complex search (`QCELS_BRACKET_EVALUATIONS`) | default maximum power 10, effective G=max(requested grid, 8*power span+1), 64 evaluations per local bracket, 32 scalar work units per complex point and evaluation | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/numerical.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/method.py` | Dimensionless finite search that keeps the original grid candidates. Operation-size bounds and grid resolution are neither coverage nor a global-optimum theorem. The search gives no QCELS uncertainty interval. The 62 golden-section iterations after two initial points shrink a bracket of two grid cells by a factor of about 1.1e-13. At the default grid the final width, about 3.4e-16, is below the binary64 spacing 4.4e-16 near pi. The work formula and the saved-fit check use at most `(1 + QCELS_BRACKET_EVALUATIONS)*G` evaluations. |
| QCELS and RFE signal resolution (`_signal_vanishes`) | count data: exact zero. Exact data: `sqrt(2)*mean_window*W + gamma_(2K)*sum_k w_k*|z_k|` for K samples with positive integer weights w_k summing to W | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/numerical.py` (`qcels`, `rfe`) | A power carries a signal only when the sum of its samples exceeds what the accepted means and the summation can produce from an exact zero. `mean_window` is the window that accepted each exact mean, `PreparedArtifact.probability_window` for exact probabilities and `NUMERICAL_RELATION_RTOL` for a classical routine, a classical computation that a backend runs in place of a circuit. The QPE Method passes it from `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/method.py::_mean_window`, the largest preparation-record window of a Run with exact probabilities, `NUMERICAL_RELATION_RTOL` for classical execution and None for count data. The rounding term follows Higham, 2002, doi:10.1137/1.9780898718027, Lemma 3.5 for the complex additions and the standard model (2.4) for the real weights. A repeated power in count data sums several means, and an exact cancellation among them can leave a roundoff residue that counts as a signal. The decision sets QCELS aliasing and flatness and RFE flatness. Revisit with those windows. |
| `QPE_INTERVAL_CONFIDENCE` | 0.95 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/numerical.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/records.py::QPEInterval.level` | Nominal level of the RWPE moment-matched Gaussian interval, mean plus or minus 1.96 standard deviations. It is a model level, not frequentist coverage, and the single-phase Gaussian model can fail for mixed spectra. Revisit together with `QPEInterval.level`, which stores the same value. |
| RWPE Gaussian prior and horizon | mean=0 radians, std=pi radians, 14 steps | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/method.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/controller.py` | A Gaussian centered on the principal phase branch with width pi is a broad starting model. Fourteen one-bit updates are an untuned finite workload. The exact model width shrinks by sqrt((e-1)/e) per step, while the basic walk has a finite exploration range. Revisit the prior and horizon together for the intended distribution of eigenphases and longest evolution time. |
| QPE automatic time | margin .9, principal radius pi, SPE cap pi/3 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/method.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/numerical.py` | The .9 factor is an untuned default that sets a nominal target with a 10 percent margin against aliasing. For a dense input of dimension D, rounding can put tau*R above .9 times the radius by a relative O(D*u), with u = 2**-53, and the margin keeps tau*abs(E) below the radius. QCELS, RFE and continuous-time RWPE use the principal spectral radius pi. SPE uses min(pi/3, pi/2-delta) when its filter transition delta is available, preventing evaluation in the periodic wrap transition. An unavailable filter proof remains visible. |
| QPE `minimum_overlap` | SPE's declared eta, otherwise .9 when omitted | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/records.py::QPEVerification` | Criterion for an explicit nominal projector comparison. SPE checks the lowest cluster, and other estimators check the largest prepared cluster. Revisit with the desired scientific target. This does not change the measurements or establish total error. |
| QPE ctrl_power_budget | 1e-3 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/method.py` | Per-power allowance for the complete structural subtotal of a product-formula power: pruning, the product formula at the represented step, represented-time displacement and identity-phase and rotation-angle formation. The numerical and synthesis error of the backend circuit remains unknown. |
| `qpe/method.py::_QPEMethod.pauli_pruning_rtol` | 1e-12 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/method.py` | Relative-to-largest-coefficient cutoff after terms are merged. Only nonidentity terms are pruned, and the identity remains in the phase. Removed L1 mass enters the allowance of the chosen power evolution. Revisit for the requested operator scale/error budget. |
| `QPEVerification.group_atol` | `None`: `1e-8*(E_max - E_min)` for a Hamiltonian target, `1e-8` radians for a unitary target | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/records.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/verification.py::_group_tolerance` | Nominal numerical clustering tolerance, in the Problem's energy unit for a Hamiltonian target and in radians of eigenphase angle for a unitary target. For a Hamiltonian it follows the scale of H and does not change under `H + cI`. A value you set is used as given. The facts `group_tolerance` and `estimated_resolved` report the tolerance used and whether it exceeds the heuristic rounding scale `D*2**-53*max|E|`. It is not a proof of exact degeneracy. |
| QPE identity phase | exact zero only | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/method.py` | Each emitted increment P(-(p_k-p_(k-1)) tau identity) between adjacent power positions of a fixed schedule, or P(-power tau identity) at an RWPE time, is kept on the control and omitted only when it is exactly zero. There is no small-phase cutoff. |
| QPE projector roundoff window | `n*eps/(1-n*eps)*max(1, norm(psi)**2)` with `n = 16*D*k` | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/verification.py::_projector_weight` | Accepts a QR projector weight slightly outside [0, 1] for D rows and k cluster vectors, with `eps = 2**-52`, twice the unit roundoff u. The first factor is therefore Higham's gamma_(2n) = 2n*u/(1-2n*u) (*Accuracy and Stability of Numerical Algorithms*, 2nd ed., SIAM 2002, doi:10.1137/1.9780898718027, Lemma 3.1). The factor 16 is an untuned allowance for the inner products, squares, cluster sum and QR orthogonality loss, not a derived bound. Revisit with a derived Householder-QR bound or if a legitimate weight is rejected. |

### QPE selected numerical controls

The shared QPE-family controls `max_power`, `max_trotter_steps` and `max_work` default to `None`, no limit, and `max_bytes` to `None`, the physical memory. When set, they bound the chosen finite schedule, the construction of the step circuits, the numerical work of each planning stage whose work grows faster than its arrays, and the arrays.

The power schedule charge, 64 bytes for each schedule entry and each SPE or RFE frequency allowance, covers those entries only, and its work grows no faster, so `max_work` does not apply to it. Estimator analysis arrays have their own check, and the Plan, query, power and Program records are not counted, so `max_bytes` does not bound them or the memory of the process, nothing refuses for them, and a very large query count can exhaust memory during planning. On sampled quantum RFE and QCELS with small Pauli inputs and the product-formula route, the traced heap peak of the whole plan was about 30 to 31 KB per logical query and about 23 to 24 KB stayed after planning (Q = 1000 and 4000 for two-qubit RFE, 2002 for two-qubit QCELS, 1000 for ten-qubit RFE, eight shots, Python 3.12.14, Qiskit 2.6.0, Aer 0.18.0, macOS, `tracemalloc`). These are whole-plan totals divided by Q. They are not sizes of isolated objects, a byte bound, a slope of process footprint or a coefficient for other routes. The query count at which memory runs out has only been forecast, and no exhaustion has been measured. Before planning, the logical query count Q is at most `2*min(S, K)` for RFE, `2*min(S, d+1)` for SPE and `2*(1 + min(T, P))` for QCELS, with S samples, K the frequency bound, d the Fourier degree, T the requested times and P the largest power. A larger `max_bytes` that lets a large schedule plan also applies to `prepare` on that Plan, and it does not make execution affordable.

The padding of a non-power-of-two input (`64*P**2` bytes for padded dimension P), the automatic choice of tau (`48*D**2` bytes) and a Suzuki step (32 bytes per term and qubit) do work of the order of their arrays and are checked against `max_bytes` only. The RWPE update after each measured bit is scalar arithmetic, and `max_steps` bounds the number of updates. A dense block counts its matrix arithmetic, D**3 units per product of two D-square matrices (`src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/powers.py::_dense_block`, with the eigendecomposition of a Hamiltonian counted by `_linalg_laws.hermitian_eigensystem_work`), and the exact synthesis of its controlled matrix on n+1 qubits (`_dense_synthesis.controlled_synthesis_size`, see the row "LCU size laws"). A unitary input's polar base is counted once per Plan, `9*D**3 + 8*D**2` work units (`src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/powers.py::polar_base`), and a unitary block's bytes include every cached binary square of that base, `(96+16J)*D**2` plus the synthesis bytes and `256(J+1)+65536`, for the largest square index J of the Plan's exponents. Each block is checked on its own, and a dense block on seven system qubits needs about 2.3e8. An explicit `max_work` bounds the counted work. The Method default `None` sets no work cap. The exact trajectory constructs one block per distinct gap between consecutive powers, and the Plan's total over its blocks is not compared with `max_work`. `src/nwqlib/subroutines/qpe/coherent.py::build_coherent_qpe_circuit` has its own `max_work`, also `None` by default, and the same `max_bytes` default, and counts for its m powers of a D-square unitary `m*D**3` work plus m times the work of `controlled_synthesis_size(log2(D)+1)`, and `96*D**2` bytes plus the working bytes of one synthesis and the kept bytes of all m, before its unitarity check. They are separate from the work a Run submits and do not bound Qiskit or LAPACK CPU/RSS. `QPEVerification` uses the same work and byte defaults for its explicit reference. It counts the eigendecomposition, `_linalg_laws.hermitian_eigensystem_work` units and `80*D**2` bytes, only when it computes one, and then the QR and projection of all eigenvalue clusters as one step, `_linalg_laws.qr_work(D, g) + 16*D*g` units for each cluster of g eigenvectors summed over the clusters, with `48*D*g` bytes for the largest. It checks each of the two steps on its own. Revisit for a concrete larger workload of the method. No implicit reference or environment work expands these defaults.

| Constant | Value | Site | Reason and revisit condition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

### QPE controlled Suzuki step law

These formulas describe the chosen synthesis model and are separate from measured compiler output, like the shared [circuit-free synthesis laws](#circuit-free-synthesis-laws). Revisit them when the construction or compiler changes, using independent matched-workload evidence.

A QPE controlled second-order Suzuki step (`src/nwqlib/algorithms/qpe/powers.py::suzuki_step_cx`) costs `4*sum_j w_j` CX, where w_j is the number of nonidentity symbols of Pauli term j. Qiskit 2.6.0's `PauliEvolutionGate.control` controls only the central RZ of each one-term rotation, so a controlled rotation of support w has a parity chain and its inverse, 2(w-1) CX, and a controlled RZ of 2 CX, and the step applies every term twice. The count is exact for transpilation to `cx,u` at optimization level 0 before routing. The step also records `2L` logical operations for L terms, and the one-qubit identity phase and feedback RZ record one operation and zero CX. The formulas of the shared common step bind its `step_time`, the formulas of an independently chosen sampled or RWPE step also bind its `power` and `steps`, and Repeat supplies the executed step count. Transpiled counts matched these formulas on 11 one-term Pauli rotations with supports one to four, each at three angles with and without control, on the identity-phase, feedback and Hadamard gates, on five chosen steps and on whole small QCELS and RFE workloads with 8 and 10 settings (Python 3.12.14 and Qiskit 2.6.0 on macOS arm64, transpiling to `cx` and `u` at optimization level 0 without a coupling map). Revisit when Qiskit changes its Pauli-evolution synthesis or `construct_suzuki_step` changes its schedule.

## QLS

### QLS `1/x` polynomial and phase pipeline

| Constant | Value | Site | Reason and revisit condition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `QLS_PHASE_RELATIVE_TARGET_SHARE` | 0.1 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qls/constants.py` | The ratio c of the default phase target of `qsvt_inverse`, `max(1e-12, c * epsilon_inv / (polynomial_kappa * rescale))`, used when `phase_tol` is `None`. When the second argument decides and the actual residual meets it, the phase term of the relative inverse error is at most `c * epsilon_inv`, because `||y|| >= 1`. `epsilon_inv` stays the polynomial's own target, so the degree does not change. An engineering ratio, neither an optimal constant nor a guarantee on the total error. Revisit if a public workload shows a material accuracy or cost change. |
| `QLS_TARGET_MARGIN` | 1e-3 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qls/constants.py` | Rescale margin over the Chebyshev norming bound, which leaves each QLS phase target with norming bound 1/(1 + 1e-3), below the strict cap of canonical Wx synthesis. At this margin all 400 `qsvt_inverse` targets on a geometric grid of `polynomial_kappa` from 1.01 to 50 and all 300 kernel-reflection targets on a grid from 1.01 to 80, at the default `epsilon_inv` 0.01, synthesize with coefficient residual at most 8.91e-13, with degrees up to 265 and 452 and transform lengths up to 32,768 and 65,536 (11 s in all, Python 3.12.14, NumPy 2.5.2 and SciPy 1.18.1 on an Apple M3 Max). The targets are built and synthesized as `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qls/host_planning.py::select_polynomial` and `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qls/quantum.py::plan_quantum` build and synthesize them. The margin costs 0.1% of success amplitude. Revisit if the norming evaluation or the synthesis cap changes. |
| `QLS_POLYNOMIAL_KAPPA_FLOOR` | 1.01 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qls/constants.py` | Separate polynomial-domain floor. The computed alpha/sigma_min and the original condition number remain unchanged, including a condition number of exactly 1. The polynomial constructions require a parameter greater than 1. Revisit if the domain requirement of either polynomial construction changes. |
| `QLS_SPECTRAL_PREMISE_RTOL` | 1e-12 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qls/constants.py` | Relative window comparing the chosen alpha/kappa with already-computed singular endpoints. Consistent with the existing dense-dilation SVD normalization guard, with no absolute floor. Raw endpoints and the chosen alpha are kept, and automatic kappa has the mathematical minimum 1. It checks numerical consistency only and is not a certified spectral or physical-error bound. Revisit with precision or spectral-estimator changes. |
| QLS conditioning remark scale | `sigma_min/sigma_max <= d*eps` | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qls/host_planning.py` (`conditioning_note`) | Decides only whether a refusal adds that the usual normwise roundoff analysis gives no relative-accuracy guarantee for `sigma_min` and that A may be singular. A heuristic diagnostic scale for the backward error of a spectral routine that certifies no LAPACK implementation. It implies no singularity cutoff and refuses nothing. Revisit with a changed spectral routine. |
| `qsp/inverse.py::_table` residual check length | `M = 4(d+2)` Chebyshev zeros for an inverse table of degree d | `src/nwqlib/subroutines/qsp/inverse.py` | The difference between the stored table and the exact minimax polynomial has degree d+1, so the Ehlich-Zeller factor at these zeros is `sec(pi(d+1)/(2M))`, below `sec(pi/8)`, about 1.082. The DCT evaluation takes O(d log d) work and O(d) storage. Revisit if the factor dominates the headroom of the residual check. |
| `_CHECK_RESOLUTION_EPS_PER_BIT` | 4 | `src/nwqlib/subroutines/qsp/inverse.py` | Untuned resolution term of the inverse residual check, `4 eps` per bit of `M`, with eps the binary64 machine epsilon. A stored table is used only when its numerical bound stays below `epsilon_inv` by its own formation term plus this term, which also covers an accidentally small or zero observed formation term. At `kappa = 43/32` and `epsilon_inv` 2e-15 above the degree-1 minimax residual 825/2873, it refines the degree-1 table to degree 3. Revisit with a different evaluation method or an arithmetic enclosure of the check. |
| `_REFINEMENT_FORMATION_RESERVE` | 4 | `src/nwqlib/subroutines/qsp/inverse.py` | When the first table misses the check, the refinement takes the smallest larger `m` whose minimax residual is at most `epsilon_inv - 2*4*F - R`, with F the first table's formation term and R the resolution term, so a refined formation term up to four times the first still passes. Formation terms are rounding noise. Over windows of 10% in `m` they varied by factors up to 3.07 for `kappa` up to 3000, and in a sweep of 25 `kappa` from 1.01 to 3000 and 300 `epsilon_inv` from 1e-14 to 0.5, the 755 refined tables reached 2.2 times the first and all passed (27 s, Python 3.12.14, NumPy 2.5.2 and SciPy 1.18.1 on an Apple M3 Max). A reserve of 1 lets the refinement at `kappa = 100`, `epsilon_inv = 1e-12` miss. Revisit with another evaluation of the table or a sweep that exceeds the factor. |
