| norm-grid trials per candidate | `ceil(100 ln(20 |T|))` | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qls/norm_search.py` | Dalzell arXiv:2406.12086v2, Sec. 5.1, log-grid detector formula. This prospective trial count is not a number of measured shots. |
| norm-sequence trials per candidate | `ceil(100 ln(20*4))*(1+log2(kappa_rounded)-j)` | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qls/norm_search.py` | Dalzell arXiv:2406.12086v2, Eq. (24), with four candidates per visited step and the Sec. 5.3 overhead. The reported `planned_trials` argument sums four times this per-candidate count over all visited steps. It is not a measured coverage guarantee. |
| `PROJECTOR_ROW_TILE` | 64 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qls/numerical.py` | Row-tile length of the rank-one update that forms `G_t` in place. It bounds the outer-product temporary at `16 r a` bytes with `r = min(a, 64)`, the `B_G` term of the classical workspace formula in `host_planning.selected_work`. Tiling changes storage, not the per-entry multiply and subtract. The value is untuned. Revisit if the rank-one temporary becomes a measured peak. |

### Explicit QLS verification criteria

These constants in `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qls/constants.py` set the numerical and heuristic windows of explicit QLS verification. They are separate from the `NUMERICAL_RELATION_RTOL` check of records and are not certified forward-error or finite-sample confidence bounds.

| Constant | Value | Site | Reason and revisit condition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `QLS_SPECTRAL_COVERAGE_TOLERANCE` | 1e-9 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qls/constants.py` | Dimensionless lower/upper spectral-domain deficit window. Preserves ordinary auto-kappa rounding. Change only with an independently justified criterion. |
| `QLS_EQ17_ABSOLUTE_WINDOW` | 2e-12 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qls/constants.py` | Absolute additive window for automatic Eq. (17) comparisons (Dalzell arXiv:2406.12086v2) after both phase endpoints are widened. It does not modify an explicitly set tolerance. Revisit with a derived roundoff bound for the Eq. (17) endpoints. |
| `QLS_INVERSE_BINOMIAL_VARIANCE_FLOOR` | 1e-12 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qls/constants.py` | Floor on the predicted per-trial Bernoulli variance before division by returned shots, so a predicted rate of exactly 0 or 1 keeps a positive shot allowance. Used only when the reference rate is in [0,1]. No coverage guarantee. Revisit if the shot term adopts a finite-sample confidence method. |

The automatic shot term is four times the predicted-rate standard deviation. Dalzell's Eq. (17) shot term (arXiv:2406.12086v2) has no variance floor, and `QLS_EQ17_ABSOLUTE_WINDOW` keeps the combined Eq. (17) allowance positive. Default shortcut direction tolerance is `5*epsilon_inv`, a heuristic. Verification records (`VerificationReceipt`) keep the raw discrepancy and the deterministic and shot allowances separately. An automatic ratio is a distinct heuristic fact. Revisit these choices through their scientific criterion, never by changing a value merely to pass a failed result.

### QLS construction and readout limits

| Constant | Value | Site | Reason and revisit condition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `qls/method.py::QLS` and `qls/verification.py::QLSVerification` limits | QLS and QLSVerification: `max_work` None (no limit) and `max_bytes` None (the physical memory) | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qls/method.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qls/verification.py` | Explicit construction and reference allowances, each checked before the work it bounds. `max_work` caps the size formulas of the original SVD or Hermitian eigendecomposition, spectral selection, dense completion, encoding construction, the exact synthesis of controlled dense queries, the classical model and the comparisons of sampled Pauli grouping, each step against the whole cap, and `max_bytes` the peak live arrays of each of these steps, of the polynomial construction and of each attempt of the phase synthesis. The exact projected reductions of one Run are counted together against `max_work`. With one control, the default `dense_control_route` synthesizes the forward and the adjoint controlled dilation, twice `controlled_synthesis_size(log2(p)+2)` for padded dimension p. With the two controls of `shortcut_dilation` it takes the gate-wise route, twice the sum of `dense_synthesis_size(log2(p)+1)` and `gatewise_control_size(*gatewise_control_counts(log2(p)+1, 2))`. An explicit `max_work=1_000_000_000` allows a controlled dense dilation of at most 64 padded coordinates (4.2e8 units with one control, 2.4e8 with two) and rejects 128 (3.2e9 and 1.4e9). The Hermitian inverse queries the dilation without controls and keeps the completion bound of at most 256 coordinates. The verification caps bound its reference solve and spectrum. They are not accuracy requirements, elapsed-time limits or RSS bounds. Revisit for a deliberately larger workload, keeping the check before the work. |
| QLS sampled count registers | `r_g` measured bits per setting, `c=max_g r_g` shared classical bits | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/qls/quantum.py::_counts_bits`, `_selection`, `_program` | A group measures its support and valued selectors. Samples and physical-prefix mass settings measure all coordinates. A table has at most `min(shots,2**r_g)` nonzero outcomes saved with c-bit keys. The generic readout check conservatively sets aside `min(shots,2**c)` CountBin JSON records with the existing prototype bound. The decoder checks zero unused suffix bits and maps each parity mask into the measured order. Its uint64 route requires `c<=64`. Distinct measurement maps add the qualified Program allowance `1024*(M_p+V+T)`, where M_p counts distinct position/wire pairs, V distinct ordered maps and T their summed lengths. The checking work of a sampled Program grows with the number of its distinct measured registers. One measured example uses the following observable. Initialize `rng = numpy.random.default_rng(11)` once, draw labels with `"".join(rng.choice(list("IXYZ"), size=8))` until 160 distinct labels other than the all-I label exist, sort them, and then draw one standard-normal coefficient per sorted label with `rng.normal()` from the same generator. With 100 shots and Plan seed 5, a `NormalizedExpectation` of this observable for `A = diag(linspace(1, 2, 256))` and `b` of standard-normal entries drawn with `default_rng(3)` forms 108 groups with 27 distinct registers, and its Program needs 130,560 units, against 60,425 when every setting measures all wires. The 160-term prefix of the sampled Expectation measurement in the `src/nwqlib/ir/records.py::Program` entry of [Record identity JSON](#record-identity-json), on `PeriodicStencil(12, mass=1.0, diffusion=0.25)` with the all-zero occupation RHS, a `NormalizedExpectation` or `QuadraticForm` output, 100 shots and Plan seed 7, forms 149 groups with 111 distinct registers, and its Program needs 339,214 units, against 82,729 when every setting measures all wires. These figures were measured with Python 3.12.14 and Qiskit 2.5.2 on macOS arm64. Revisit when the count representation, layout or Program object convention changes. |

## GCiM

### ADAPT-GCiM defaults and tolerances

| Constant | Value | Site | Reason and revisit condition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ADAPT-GCiM `theta` default | pi/4 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt.py` | Fixed angle in Zheng et al. (2024), arXiv:2312.07691v3, Methods. Explicit optional BFGS may later change it, including to zero. |
| ADAPT-GCiM `t_auto_fraction` / `t_user` | 0.20 / 10 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_acquisition.py` | Zheng et al., arXiv:2312.07691v3, Methods, uses `T=min(0.2*unselected_count, T_usr)`. An integer flat count reaches a fractional T at its ceiling. The default user cap 10 is the paper's H4 setting and is adjustable for other pools. With max_iterations=8, a pool larger than 42 members cannot reach this flat threshold. The ADAPT loop checks the iteration cap first and stops at the eighth selection, so the last flat check follows the seventh. The flat count is then at most seven, and the stop needs 0.2*(P-7) <= 7 for a pool of P members (`src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_acquisition.py::_stopping_reason`, GCiM guide). Count small changes from the reference energy and reset the count after a large or unavailable change. This heuristic is not a physical error certificate. |
| ADAPT-GCiM `max_iterations` | 8 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_acquisition.py::_stopping_reason` | Finite cap on selection rounds, which bounds the working basis at `2*min(8, pool size)` states and the number of pool screenings. Zheng et al., arXiv:2312.07691v3, Methods, stop by the flat counter and state no iteration cap. Measured with Python 3.12.14 on macOS arm64 with exact evaluation, the spin-adapted pool and θ = π/4, eight selections reach chemical accuracy for both of the following molecules. The H4 chain of the eigenvalue example (2 Å, 8 qubits, 66 pool members) reaches FCI to roundoff at the eighth selection and is 14 mHa above it after the seventh. LiH at 1.6 Å with active space (2, 5) (10 qubits, 160 members) is 0.46 mHa above FCI after the eighth selection and at FCI after the ninth. With the cap at 32 the flat counter stops them at selections 18 and 19, ten after each energy reached FCI, with 36 and 38 basis states instead of 16. For H4 under quantum execution with exact probabilities, that raises the pair circuits from 136 to 666 (formula of the eigenvalue notebook, Appendix A). With `t_user=10` the cap keeps the flat counter from stopping pools larger than 42 members (row above). The cap does not certify convergence. Revisit when a default workload needs more than eight selections or when the flat counter should stop larger pools by default. |
| ADAPT `BoundedRitzGrowth` `parent_window` / `candidates_per_round` / `max_candidate_scans` | 4 / 4 / 256 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/bounded_growth.py::frontier` | Limits on the work of one bounded-growth round, with no recorded derivation or tuning. A round extends the reference and at most three chains added last, evaluates at most four extensions, whose matrix elements take at most `4(b + 1)` new pairs of states for a basis of b states, and examines at most 256 extensions, which costs chain comparisons and, under `basis_depth_limit`, one read of each generator circuit. They are not convergence parameters, and a stop at the scan or parent limit says nothing about extensions that no round examined. Revisit when measured workloads show that a wider window or more scored extensions per round lower the energy more per obtained matrix element. |
| ADAPT `BoundedRitzGrowth.independence_cutoff` | None, which uses ADAPT's `overlap_cutoff` (default 1e-12) | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt.py::independence_floor`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/bounded_growth.py::candidate_score` | Exact floor on eta, the squared norm of an extension's part outside the kept overlap subspace, at or below which the extension is not scored. It takes over the overlap cutoff's numerical null-space convention, which has no recorded derivation, and gives no protection against sampling noise. With shots, the margin of the sampled eligibility rule (next row) must exceed it. Revisit when exact runs near linear dependence need a separate floor. |
| ADAPT `BoundedRitzGrowth` sampled eligibility: multiplier, metric limit and one-count floor | k = 3 (`ELIGIBILITY_MULTIPLIER`), `epsilon < 1/2`, variance proxy `max(V, 1/(N + 1)**2)` | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/bounded_growth.py::ELIGIBILITY_MULTIPLIER`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/bounded_growth.py::overlap_proxy`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/bounded_growth.py::sampled_metric`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/bounded_growth.py::sampled_eligibility` | Fixed before any data are seen and not fitted to results. k = 3 multiplies the spreads formed from the variance proxies (`sigma`, `sqrt(nu_S)` and `sqrt(b_s)`), and it is not a coverage probability. `epsilon < 1/2` keeps the amplification of `(I + D)^(-1)` at most 2 in the rule's second-order model, and it is not a cutoff on eta. `1/(N + 1)` is a chosen scale for the resolution of one count among outcomes in [-1, 1], so that a setting whose outcomes were all equal still counts as noisy, and it is not a lower bound on the variance. The rule is a conditional empirical policy, not a confidence interval or an independence certificate. Revisit when measured sampled workloads show that it marks resolvable extensions unresolved or admits extensions whose sampled score is dominated by noise, or when a qualified statistical rule replaces it. |
| ADAPT-GCiM default `energy_tol` / `gradient_norm_floor` (`DEFAULT_ENERGY_CHANGE_PER_SCALE`, `DEFAULT_GRADIENT_FLOOR_PER_SCALE`) | 1e-6 Omega / 1e-8 Omega | multipliers: `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_inputs.py`. Resolved in `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt.py`, read in `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_acquisition.py` | Flat-counter change tolerance and surrogate gradient-norm exit floor, each with the energy unit of H. `Omega = ||H - tr(H) I/d||_F / sqrt(d)` on the original dimension d (`energy_scale`) is a defined scale, chosen because it is the same quantity for Pauli, dense and sparse input, multiplies by a under `H -> aH + bI` for a > 0, and costs one linear scan. It can grow with the number of terms. The multipliers are heuristic, and neither threshold establishes total output accuracy. An explicit value is used as given, as an absolute value in the unit of H. Revisit when a workload shows a stop that depends on system size, or when better-supported stopping evidence replaces the flat counter. |
| ADAPT explicit diagnostic `residual_tolerance` | No default | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_verification.py` | Absolute tolerance in the energy unit for the post-hoc residual classification `residual_threshold_met`, `pool_limited` and `stalled`, which are reported as unavailable without it. No number suits every unit, and a tolerance relative to the energy scale would change what accuracy is asked for. It does not change when the ADAPT loop stops or the accuracy of the requested output. SHOTS does not claim the deterministic spectral interval. |
| ADAPT BFGS `BFGS_NORMALIZED_GTOL` | 1e-5 on the dimensionless objective | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/optimization.py` | Stationarity heuristic with no user control. BFGS on `(E - c)/Omega` stops at `max_j abs(dE/dtheta_j) <= 1e-5 Omega`, which is covariant under `H -> aH + bI` for a > 0. It does not bound the residual or the ground-energy error, and optimization can end before the test is met. The physical threshold grows with Omega, so it is not a local precision independent of system size. No derivation makes the value optimal, and no molecular run calibrates it, so a smaller value need not improve the final Ritz energy and can cost more evaluations. With shots it applies to the estimated gradient. It is passed explicitly so that it does not follow a later SciPy default. Revisit when an optimized workload shows a stop that depends on system size, or when users need a different stationarity target, whose meaning would be `gtol*Omega` per angle. |
| ADAPT `symmetry_tol` | 1e-12 | default: `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt.py`. Check: `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_inputs.py` | Relative Frobenius correction window for the anti-Hermitian projection `(A - A^dagger)/2` of each pool generator. The Hamiltonian gets no window and must already be exactly Hermitian. Its Pauli coefficients must be real, and the classical matrix route keeps the original Hermitian matrix. Zero requires an exactly anti-Hermitian generator. The applied correction is recorded and the processed generator is the target. Because the window is relative, a generator with a large Frobenius norm, for example from an identity term, allows a larger absolute correction. Revisit for changed precision or an application requiring an absolute correction budget. |
| ADAPT default `pauli_cutoff` (`DEFAULT_PAULI_CUTOFF_PER_SCALE`) | 1e-14 Omega | multiplier and H/commutator preparation: `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_inputs.py`. Resolved in `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt.py` | Approximation in the operator's units, with Omega as in the row of `energy_tol`. After duplicate-label accumulation and symmetry projection, keep a real nonidentity coefficient iff `abs(c) > cutoff`, and keep the identity coefficient whole, so that a shift by bI moves the energy by b on either side of the cutoff. An explicit value is used as given, as an absolute value, and zero disables nonzero-term pruning. This is not a universal accuracy guarantee, and the small size of a coefficient does not show that it is only rounding. The reported `hamiltonian_dropped_l1` bounds the removed operator's norm and the energy change on a fixed state or fixed trial subspace, not the error of the final adaptive energy. Choose an explicit value relative to target accuracy. |
| ADAPT supplied-reference comparison tolerance | explicitly required positive `reference_tolerance` | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_verification.py` | No automatic THEORY/STATEVECTOR/SHOTS reference tolerance. The requested comparison checks the recorded processed Ritz scalar against a supplied framed scalar in their declared unit. It does not establish ground-state identity or total output accuracy. |
| GCiM sampled enclosure diagnostic (`SAMPLED_PENCIL_ENCLOSURE_RTOL`) | 1e-8 relative | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/fixed_basis.py`, `src/nwqlib/_projected_eigensolver.py` | Compare the finite sampled Ritz value with the processed Pauli L1 enclosure. Scale the distance by the largest endpoint magnitude and keep its absolute distance. A violation is a plausibility diagnostic, does not stop the ADAPT loop or veto previous-energy/flat-counter updates, and is not a statistical or spectral certificate. A refusal by the solver itself is separate. Revisit for changed precision or conditioning. |
| `gcim/sector.py` | imaginary-part window `1e-10` | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/sector.py` | Untuned absolute acceptance window for a spin expectation on the already normalized input. Rescaling the original state does not change this expectation. It is not a dimension-uniform arithmetic bound. Revisit condition: Larger spin sectors, precision or a derived summation-error policy. |
| ADAPT Taylor-model gradient regression tolerance | absolute 2e-11, relative 0 | `tests/test_adapt_shift_science.py` | Compares `optimization.normalized_taylor_energy_gradient` for iX then iZ at angles 3/16 and -5/16, a zero first angle and degree 1, with exact rational ascending-power Taylor matrices on a unit-scale two-level example. It is a small-case regression threshold, not a bound for arbitrary chains or a derivative of the step-count selector. |

### Fermionic generators and chemistry input

| Constant | Value | Site | Reason and revisit condition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `GENERATOR_COEFFICIENT_CUTOFF` | 1e-12 absolute | `src/nwqlib/subroutines/fermionic_pool.py` | Removes binary64 residues of exactly cancelling terms after all contributions to a normal-ordered term or Pauli label are summed. The smallest kept Pauli coefficient is 0.0255 in the six-orbital default pool and at least 0.088 in the UCCSD, QEB and CEO pools of six occupied and six virtual spin orbitals. Revisit for a generator family whose genuine coefficients can approach the cutoff, or for another precision. |
| Generator occupation planning limits | at most 8 active modes and block dimension 8 | `src/nwqlib/subroutines/fermionic_pool.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/optimization.py` | Bounded local spectrum planning for default generators. Shared-index circuit compilation separately allows at most 6 modes and dimension 5. Revisit only with a new supported generator family and its cost model. |
| Generator repeated-eigenvalue merging | `16 * eps * max_block_dimension * spectral_scale` | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/optimization.py` | Engineering window for numerical splitting of repeated eigenvalues in bounded Hermitian blocks. This is not a certified eigenvalue error bound. Revisit if a new generator family has smaller distinct spectral gaps. |
| `fermionic_pool.py`, `GENERATOR_TAYLOR_DEGREE` | 18 and `ceil(2*abs(theta)*sum(abs(c)))` steps | `src/nwqlib/subroutines/fermionic_pool.py` | The Pauli triangle bound gives step norm at most 1/2. Each exact-arithmetic Taylor remainder is at most `exp(.5)*.5**19/19! < 2.6e-23`. Accumulated step error and floating-point arithmetic need separate accounting. No operator-norm solve selects the steps. `src/nwqlib/subroutines/fermionic_pool.py::_taylor_steps` computes the count for both the exponential and ADAPT's classical work formulas (`src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_records.py::_exponential_action_work`), so the two cannot differ by a step. Revisit condition: Different precision, larger step counts or a requested total action-error bound. |
| `gcim/adapt.py::generator_pool_slot_bound` | `B(q,T) = max(6q*max(1,T), 24q+6192)` logical gate slots (gate positions counted before cancellation) per generator, or `6q*max(1,T)` when every pool generator has commuting terms | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt.py` | Family-level upper bound for the routes of `fermionic_circuits.build_generator_circuit`, with q system qubits and the largest Pauli term count T of the pool: 6q per commuting term, `5(2q+11)` for pair/split, `6(2q+516)` and `12(2q+516)` for singlet and triplet four-distinct doubles, and at most `24q+2544` for the shared-index route with at most six active modes and blocks of dimension five. The quantum query bound is `2*W_reference + 2*l*(1+B) + 8q + 7` with `l = min(max_iterations, pool size)`. It counts appended logical instructions, not the backend's synthesis, and does not establish support for an arbitrary custom noncommuting generator, which planning checks separately. Revisit condition: A new compiler route or supported generator family. |
| GCiM chemistry `coefficient_cutoff` default | 1e-12 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/chemistry.py` | The library's explicit absolute cutoff in Hartree on the fermionic terms and the final Pauli coefficients of `build_gcim_chemistry_problem`. The fermionic operator is summed with OpenFermion's `FermionOperator` addition and its Jordan–Wigner transform accumulates the Pauli terms the same way, and that addition also applies OpenFermion's own tolerance. OpenFermion 1.8.1 deletes a term whenever its running coefficient has absolute value below 1e-8 (`EQ_TOLERANCE`). A stored Pauli coefficient can therefore differ from the sum of its integral contributions by those deletions, and the library states no bound on that difference. Revisit when the builder sums its terms without OpenFermion's addition, or when OpenFermion changes its tolerance. |
| `ORBITAL_PHASE_RELATIVE_THRESHOLD` | 1e-8 relative | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/chemistry.py` | Sign convention for RHF orbitals: the first AO coefficient whose magnitude exceeds this fraction of the orbital's largest magnitude is made positive. Coefficients that vanish by symmetry carry roundoff near 1e-16 of that scale, far below the threshold, so the chosen coefficient and its sign do not depend on the LAPACK build. Fixed-angle ADAPT-GCiM depends on these signs. Rotations within degenerate orbitals are not fixed. Revisit for molecules with degenerate orbitals or a changed precision. |
| H2 chemistry-table cross-check tolerance | 1e-8 | `tests/test_gcim_chemistry.py` | High-precision hardcoded H2 electronic Jordan–Wigner table at R = 0.7414 Angstrom. Pipeline comparison subtracts nuclear repulsion from the identity term before comparing electronic coefficients. |

### GCiM work and byte limits

| Constant | Value | Site | Reason and revisit condition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `gcim/adapt.py::BoundedRitzGrowth.max_basis_size` | 64 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt.py` | Untuned stop of bounded Ritz growth, which ends the growth when the basis holds this many states. It does not establish convergence or bound Qiskit workspace. Revisit condition: An explicitly intended larger bounded basis. |
| `gcim/fixed_basis.py::FixedGCIM`, `ADAPT` and `AdaptVerificationOptions` limits | `max_basis_size=64` and `max_analysis_work=None` (row "Sampled FixedGCIM analysis admission") of FixedGCIM, and `max_products=None` and `max_bytes=None`, the physical memory, for all three | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/fixed_basis.py` | Untuned workload limits, checked before pair expansion, the grouped analysis pass, the projected solve, classical actions or verification. They bound derived counts, not CPU time, and establish no accuracy. ADAPT residual and sector verification of the built circuits counts for each decomposed k-qubit gate of the circuits it simulates `1 + 4**k + 2**q * (2**k + 2)` operations on q qubits. Whether an explicit cap allows it depends on those circuits and the reference. With an occupation reference, a residual check of any selection of up to eight spin-adapted generators at 8 qubits needs at most 91.1 million operations before the Hamiltonian action, which adds the shared Pauli action formula (`pauli_action_requirements`). The `max_products=None` default sets no product cap. Full-state checks at 20 to 25 qubits must stay an explicit choice. Revisit condition: An explicitly intended larger basis, number of matrix elements or classical workload. |
| Fixed GCiM projected size law | `512*B*B+256*E` bytes and `32*B*B+16*B*B*B` projected work | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/pencil.py` (`_projected_solve_bytes`, `_projected_solve_work`), used by `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/fixed_basis.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt.py` and `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_acquisition.py` | B basis states and E FixedGCIM settings of the Plan (exact pair reductions, or grouped sampled settings), checked before expansion. `512*B*B` is an untuned allowance of 32 complex128 B-by-B arrays for the projected solve, and `256*E` is FixedGCIM's untuned record allowance for each setting, not a derived Python-heap bound. A sampled Plan adds `G*n+8L` logical basis and index bytes for its group description. ADAPT has no matrix-element settings in this formula. It checks both formulas, the work against `max_products`, for its largest basis of `2*max_selections` states at planning (`src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt.py`), so a run that might stop at a smaller basis is still rejected when that largest solve does not fit. It checks both again before each projected analysis and each reanalysis with a new cutoff (`src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_acquisition.py::_matrix_from_observations`). It gives no CPU/RSS guarantee. |
| Classical FixedGCIM and ADAPT identity offset (`_mean_diagonal`, `_offset_free_requirements`, `_shifted_sparse`) | `c = trace(A)/d` removed before the projection. Extra work `d*d + d` for dense input. For CSR/CSC input with z stored entries, h missing diagonals and index itemsize I, persistent bytes `16z` when h = 0 and the pattern is shared, else `(16 + I)(z + h) + I(d + 1)`, plus the vectorized scan and union scratch, setup work `W_setup = 4z + 6d + 3h` and `n_s*h` extra action work for n_s columns. Before the scan h is bounded by d | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/_eigen_inputs.py::_mean_diagonal`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/fixed_basis.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_inputs.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_acquisition.py::_offset_free_column` | The mean diagonal is the mean eigenvalue, so `||A - cI||_2 <= lambda_max - lambda_min` whatever the identity offset of A, and the solve adds c back after the ill-conditioned projection. An exactly constant diagonal is its own mean, because the rounded sum of d quotients can miss it by a unit in the last place and leave rounding as a nonidentity part of H = cI. Dense row blocks are refilled in one buffer of at most n_s rows. A Run creates its shifted canonical CSR/CSC payload once, shares its original pattern only when every diagonal is present, and counts that payload against the byte limit throughout later actions. ADAPT keeps it in the Run's context for every later action. The ADAPT per-query count includes the setup work conservatively. The docstrings of `_offset_free_requirements` and `_shifted_sparse` state the formulas. Revisit when the classical projection routine or the sparse representation changes. |
| Exact FixedGCIM and ADAPT pair overlap allowance (`_exact_overlap_allowance`) and pair reduction (`pair_reducer.pair_work`, `pair_bytes`, `TILE = 1024`) | Allowance `max_i sum_(j != i) E^S_ij` from each off-diagonal pair's preparation-record saved-state error (`PreparedArtifact.saved_state_error`), unavailable when any is unresolved. The row sums are accumulated exactly and converted upward. Work `(L+F) + 3L + N(L+F+1) + N(1+L) + 2N` per pair (N for L = 0), bytes `17N + max(9L, 96t) + 254L + 65536` with `F = min(L, N)` and `t = min(N, 1024)` | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/pair_reducer.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/fixed_basis.py`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_acquisition.py` | The work covers the attempted grouped pass, the ordered retry set aside for it, group construction and the two complex contractions. The bytes cover one action output and a finite mask, the larger of group construction and tile scratch, the grouping containers of `apply_terms` and the fixed heap allowance. On CPython 3.12, with 64-bit flip keys and one group per term, tracemalloc measured these containers, a dict from each flip key to a list of member indices and a list of rotated complex coefficients, at most 254 bytes per term from 6 to 300,000 terms and at most 1,282 bytes in all for 1 to 5 terms (row Projected reduction workspace), so the heap allowance covers the small-term excess. Fewer groups hold fewer lists and dict entries, so F does not enter the bytes. The strided branches are views, and the saved state and the Plan's resident Pauli table are covered by their own checks. FixedGCIM checks its summed pair work against an explicit `max_products` at preparation, before the first pair's backend work, ADAPT checks each matrix stage's new pair work against an explicit `max_products` before the stage's first query, and both check each point's workspace against `max_bytes` before the circuits run. The tile length is an engineering choice, not a scientific threshold. A tracemalloc measurement of a 12-qubit reduction with 1000 terms in about 900 flip groups stays below this bound (`tests/test_gcim_production.py`). Revisit when the grouping of `apply_terms` or the Python version changes. |
| ADAPT packed classical Pauli actions | B_A=33d+254L+1282+max(9L,96t), W_A=2dL+dg+2d+4L+g, g=min(L,d), t=min(d,1024) | src/nwqlib/operators/_pauli.py::pauli_action_requirements, src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_records.py, src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_acquisition.py | q is the system-qubit count, d=2**q, and L counts the action's rows including zeros and duplicates. The single-column formula uses complex128 states and includes preprocessing, the grouped pass and a possible ordered retry. H0, generator and insertion rows are packed once per classical Plan. Let L0 count the nonidentity Hamiltonian rows and T_j the rows of generator j. For R=L0+2 sum_j T_j newly packed rows, their logical payload is 32R bytes, construction sets aside 64R beside other live storage, and packing counts R(q+4) pass units. Input tables and Python headers are checked separately. Revisit if masks, the copies of input tables or the action workspace change. |
| ADAPT Taylor and derivative action work | W_exp=s[d+18(W_A+2d)]+10d, with s from _taylor_steps | src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_records.py::_exponential_action_work, _energy_gradient_work | Each Horner action uses the shared packed Pauli allowance. Derivative Horner terms have two actions and reverse terms have one. The energy/gradient formula sums the complete forward, normalization, tangent, reverse and Hamiltonian work. Invocation work is checked as a whole before execution. Packing is a separate once-per-Plan count. These are logical pass allowances, not CPU-time or accuracy bounds. |
| ADAPT classical action workspace | 16(C+12)d+24ell vector/scalar bytes for any query, plus packed tables, the full-H payload, max_A(B_A-32d), reference and offset-free setup storage | src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt.py::classical_frontier_bytes, src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_verification.py | ell=max_selections, d=2**q on q system qubits, and C the most vectors the state and H-column caches hold together under `_keep_current` (`adapt.cached_state_vectors`): 8ell for the default growth and 2(B+2ell) for bounded growth with at most B states. C counts the two chains of any legal pair point, which `Plan.resolve` and `prepare_experiment` accept beside the controller's own queries, at most 4ell or about 2(B+1). A pair query holds two chains, at most C cached vectors and 12 working vectors. An energy query holds one chain, at most C-2ell cached vectors, the same working vectors and, for a chain of k<=ell generators, k forward states beyond the reference, which the cache count already holds, k tangents and 24k scalar bytes of its gradient. Every classical Plan can run an energy query, with or without `optimize_every_m`, so planning checks 16(C+12)d+24ell once against `max_bytes` before any query, and no query repeats the check. The live-vector term already contains the action input/output vectors. The remaining shared allowance includes the finite mask, action metadata and maximum preprocessing/retry scratch. Verification uses its own count of streamed live vectors and adds the action remainder for each applicable classical generator or residual-H action, along with saved arrays and Qiskit gate matrices where applicable. | Sampled FixedGCIM groups and moments | 2(M-D)max(1,G)+DG settings for a measured pencil with both parts measured, (M-D)max(1,G)+DG with real parts only, shots per setting | src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/fixed_basis.py sampled planning and group analysis | M is the required pair count including D diagonal pairs, G is the number of nonempty QWC groups, and n is the system-qubit count. Groups use separate X/Y ancilla settings off diagonal and system-only measurements on a diagonal. The current whole-register construction has c=n+1 shared classical bits, with an unused zero phase bit on diagonals. The generic CountBin JSON check sets aside min(shots,2**c) bins. Group analysis checks the sum of the decoded arrays cached on all observation chunks and the largest live moment workspace under its logical payload formula. Physical-H variance includes the overlap–Hamiltonian sample-mean covariance of the designated overlap group. One-shot variances are unavailable. Only the pooled pair variances (`SampledPairVariance`) flag a negative raw estimate, in `variance_negative`, and they keep it unclipped. The sample-mean variances of a group (`GroupMoment`) are sums of products of two numbers of the same sign, so they cannot be negative in binary64, while its covariance keeps its sign. Binary64 moment fields require finite representable moments. The scalar-identity shortcut measures no pencil. Revisit when the measured-register representation, count codec, reduction schedule or pooling rule changes. |
| Sampled FixedGCIM analysis admission | `W_plan=K sum_g W_g(m)+Lc+32b²+16b³` and `B_plan=8(w+1)KGm+112m+8max_g L_g+8KL+(n+16)L+512b²` | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/fixed_basis.py::grouped_pass_requirements`, `FixedGCIM.plan`, `read_groups` | For b trial states, n system qubits, c=n+1 classical bits, w=ceil(c/64), G nonempty QWC groups with sizes L_g, L=sum_g L_g and s shots per setting, put m=min(s,2**c). Each group occurs K times, K=b² when both parts are measured and K=b(b+1)/2 with real parts only, and the settings cover P=b(b+1)/2 pairs. The one-table work is W_g(m)=m[c+44+(4w+4)L_g]+L_g[(c-1)w+1]. The small records that pooling and pair assembly keep for each setting and pair are not counted: with P≤b(b+1)/2 pairs they stay below the projected solve's 512b² bytes, and they approach the count arrays only when the bins are so few that the pass is small. Planning checks W_plan against max_analysis_work and B_plan against max_bytes before choosing the circuit blocks. The common readout check enforces m on returned entries, including zero-count bins. The bound covers one execution per setting and conservatively includes the unused diagonal phase bit. Each analysis pass checks, before decoding, the law of `grouped_pass_requirements` for its own population of C chunks with L_a terms and m_a bins: B=sum_a 8(w+1)m_a+max_a(112m_a+8L_a)+8T+(n+16)L+512b²+8oT and W=sum_a W_a(m_a)+L(n+1)+32b²+16b³+oT, with T=sum_a L_a and o=1 when the pass builds a second, oriented copy of every group moment, as ADAPT does. With one chunk of m bins per setting and o=0 this law gives the planning formulas, and `FixedGCIM.plan` evaluates it with one (L_g, m) descriptor per group repeated for the K settings of the group (`copies=K`), so planning takes O(G) steps. ADAPT checks it against its max_bytes and max_products for every pass over its current basis, cached moments included, and before a matrix stage acquires anything it charges min(s,2**c) bins to each table not yet acquired. Under `PencilSampling`, ADAPT checks the stage-budget form on the basis that its growth ended with, before the first pilot circuit. The allowance covers the decoded arrays of the pass's chunks, one reduction workspace, the reduced group moments, the oriented copy when built, the labels and the projected solve. It is a logical model of these arrays and passes, not the process's resident memory, and it covers neither other passes nor the Run's history. ADAPT releases the cached moments of chains outside its current basis and active queries (`adapt_acquisition._keep_current`). Bytes are logical cached-array, workspace and scalar payloads, and work uses the counting convention of the group reducer's routine and passes. Saved JSON, Program records and grouping have separate checks. The scalar-identity shortcut has no grouped pass. The bound is conservative. `max_analysis_work` sets no limit by default and applies when it is given. On the three-qubit fixture of `tests/test_gcim_production.py::test_grouped_analysis_is_admitted_before_setting_construction` (18 settings at 64 shots) it plans 21,696 units, and on the 14-qubit fixture of `test_default_analysis_work_admits_a_fourteen_qubit_grouped_plan` in the same file, 80 random Pauli words (G = 60), three product states and 540 settings, it plans 154,104,240 units at 4,096 shots and 308,195,760 at 8,192, both above 10**8. Sampled FixedGCIM checks the projected solve and grouped analysis against `max_analysis_work`, and the QWC grouping against `max_bytes`. For basis size b, L nonidentity terms, n system qubits and s shots per setting, write c=n+1, w=ceil(c/64) and m=min(s,2^c). The grouped-analysis work is K{m[(c+44)G+(4w+4)L]+L[(c−1)w+1]}+Lc+32b²+16b³, where G is the number of groups formed. Before grouping, planning checks the maximum of the projected-solve work and the G=1 analysis floor against the common limit. The later grouped-analysis check uses the group and table sizes that arise, so this floor does not predict a budget that will pass every subsequent stage (`src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/fixed_basis.py::FixedGCIM.plan`). Revisit when the number of measured executions, the cardinality rule, the layouts or the reduction lifetimes change, or when pooling keeps arrays that grow with the bins. |
| `PencilSampling` per-setting stage minimum | 2 | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/pencil_sampling.py` (`pilot_split`, `uniform_allocation`, `floored_allocation`), and the lower bound of the `shots` parameter of a stage setting in `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/fixed_basis.py::_sampled_construction` | Derived requirement, not a precision choice: two requested shots in each of the pilot and the main stage give every setting a defined sample variance when they are returned. Planning therefore refuses `total_shots` below 4m for the m settings of a FixedGCIM or QFD Plan. It does not guarantee how many shots a backend returns. Revisit if the variance estimate of a setting changes. |
| `PencilSampling.pilot_fraction` | Required caller value in (0, 1), no default | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/fixed_basis.py::PencilSampling`, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/pencil_sampling.py::pilot_split` | The pilot stage takes `B_p = min(B - 2m, max(2m, floor(f B)))` of the B = `total_shots` shots, with the exact rational value of the binary64 f, and the main stage `B - B_p`, so both stages keep the minimum above. The caller chooses the share, as for the Lanczos pilot, and no measured comparison has qualified a default. Revisit if one does. |
| Stage budget of `PencilSampling` analysis | FixedGCIM and QFD: one stage of `S = max(B_p, B - B_p)` shots, at planning. ADAPT: `B >= 4m*` at planning, then one stage of `S = max(B_p, B - B_p)` shots on the final basis when growth stops | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/fixed_basis.py::grouped_pass_requirements` (`shot_total=S`) and `_pencil_admission`, which `FixedGCIM.plan` and `QFD.plan` call, `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt.py::_admit_final_stages` and `_admit_final_analysis`, which `adapt_acquisition.drive_adapt` calls | Derived bounds. The main-stage shots are unknown before the pilot, so the grouped analysis of one stage is checked with the larger stage budget, before any stage shot, against `max_analysis_work` and `max_bytes`, or for ADAPT `max_products` and `max_bytes`, by default the physical memory. FixedGCIM and QFD know their basis at planning. ADAPT knows its final basis only when growth stops. Its planning requires `B >= 4m*` for the m* settings of the largest basis it can reach, `2*min(max_iterations, pool size)` states or `min(max_basis_size, max_iterations + 1)` with bounded growth, because B cannot change once growth has spent shots. The Run then checks the analysis for the m settings of the final basis, each table with `min(S, 2**(n+1))` bins on n system qubits, before the first pilot circuit. The check can refuse a budget whose actual allocation would fit. Revisit when the stage structure or the grouped-pass law changes. |
| Final-stage room of a `PencilSampling` Run | 2m more circuit preparations, 2m more circuit runs and B more shots | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/pencil_workflow.py::_state` (FixedGCIM and QFD), `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_acquisition.py::drive_adapt` (ADAPT) | Derived count of the two stages, one preparation and one run per setting and stage, checked against the Run's `ExecutionLimits` before the first pilot circuit. With m = 0 no shot is counted. ADAPT knows m only when its growth stops, so a Run without this room is refused then, with the growth shots spent, and continues after `run.extend_limits(...)`. Revisit when a stage measures a setting more than once. |
| `gcim/adapt_verification.py::verify` | `B_states + reference_action_bytes + 16*4**k_max + B_other + B_saved` bytes | `src/nwqlib/algorithms/gcim/adapt_verification.py` | Count of the known buffers of the streamed residual and sector checks, made before they run. `B_states = 16d*max(F(l), 8*[residual]) + d`, or `B_sector + d` when larger for sector checks, with `F(0) = 3`, `F(1) = 5` and `F(l) = max(6, l+2)` full vectors for l selections (`_state_frontier`). The residual allowance of 8 vectors is an explicitly identified allowance, not a measured minimal peak. `B_sector = 16d*(1+[residual]) + 24d + B_S2` counts the Ritz state and a computed residual, both still held, the normalized copy and probabilities of `sector_expectations`, and `B_S2 = 16d + max(40d, B_ladder, 64d)` for `apply_spin_squared` (its index array and S_z diagonal, then the largest of its diagonal temporaries, the ladder product's own law and its final sum), which its own check also uses. The bit arrays of the diagonal are released before the ladder action, and the verification builds one ladder pair for its count, the action's count and the action. `16*4**k_max` covers the widest Qiskit gate matrix of the built circuit, from the gate count that gives the evolution work, `B_other` is the shared action remainder `max_A(B_A-32d)` of the ADAPT classical action workspace row, with the packed tables, on a classical Plan, or that of the H action when a quantum Plan requests residual verification, and `B_saved` adds once the distinct arrays the saved Result already holds. Python object headers, allocator overhead and Qiskit decomposition storage are excluded, and the reference synthesis keeps its own check. One running work total covers every pool member against `max_products`, and the byte check includes completed member tables (`8J_i(q+1)` each), the current surviving products and their sort, conversion and copy workspace. The tile length is an engineering choice, not a scientific threshold. These are upper bounds for accounting, not exact elapsed-work counts or heap bounds.
